    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-packet-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "On a packet diagram after the radial stage, choices of smooth cutoffs and approximations can be made from deeper faces to cofaces, retaining exact products outside finite thresholds. More precisely, fix a finite derivative order and, on each packet, positive error tolerances for any finite collection of smooth data or approximations. A datum on a face may additionally be required to meet the tolerances of all its incident cofaces, including through chains. These requirements can be replaced in advance by strictly positive face tolerances, and then met by compatible blending, provided the individual local approximations and their required convergence to the face data are available. In particular there exist compatible smooth positive functions arbitrarily small in any prescribed finite derivative norm on each packet. Also, if compatible closed sets $F_E$ and compatible open sets $O_E$ satisfy $F_E\\subset O_E$ on every packet, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_E(F_E,E\\setminus O_E)>0$ on each fixed packet whenever both sets are nonempty.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-packet-reconstruction",
        "exact_quote": "Starting with $X$, perform finitely many compatible regular splits as in <a href=\"#packet:split\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"packet:split\">31</a>. For the first split require locally finite compact radial wall and side components. At the later split for color $i$, require the defining functions to be positive on the inverse image of $A_i$ and negative on the inverse image of $B_i$, with those inverse images contained in open regions of the stipulated signs. After any $r\\ge1$ of these splits, the diagram has a reconstruction $X_r$, obtained by finite collar truncations and gluing at hit faces, with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$X_r$ is a smooth manifold homeomorphic to $X$. Its $r$ labelled walls are closed smooth hypersurfaces with simultaneous transverse intersection charts. Each wall component is compact, the component families are locally finite, and the closed sign/hit pieces are exactly the compact cornered truncations of the analytic packets, by components.*\n\n\n2.  *The packet maps glue to a proper continuous map $q_r:X_r\\to X$. There is a homeomorphism $H_r:X_r\\to X$ such that $$\\label{packet:displacement}\n     d_0\\bigl(H_r(z),q_r(z)\\bigr)\\le r\\qquad(z\\in X_r).$$*\n\n3.  *Horizons can be enlarged to exceed any already specified compatible product thresholds on the full analytic packets. A horizon is constant for all occurrences of a normal coordinate belonging to one connected global wall component. Such enlargement has an identification with the previous reconstruction which preserves $q_r$, every old wall, and every old side status.*\n\n4.  *Once color $i$ has been processed, $q_r^{-1}(A_i)$ remains in its open positive phase and $q_r^{-1}(B_i)$ remains in its open negative phase at every subsequent stage. In particular its wall misses $q_r^{-1}(A_i\\cup B_i)$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-cut-pressure",
        "exact_quote": "There is a smooth finite compatible function $p_0$ and a number $c_*>0$ such that every $p=p_0+c$, $|c|<c_*$, satisfies $$\\label{cut:pressure-gradient}\n             2|\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}p|\\le .67p^2+1.02\\Pi_{j_0}.$$ There are compatible smooth barrier domains $D^+,D^-$ whose interiors contain the corresponding forced-core closures and whose closures lie in the respective pole interiors. Each admits a smooth vector field $Y^\\pm$ with $|Y^\\pm|\\le1$, equal to the outward unit normal on its boundary, such that, for all these shifts, $$\\label{cut:barrier-pressure}\n p\\ge1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\omega Y^+\\quad\\hbox{on }D^+,\n \\qquad\n -p\\ge1+\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_\\omega Y^-\\quad\\hbox{on }D^-.$$ After the radial split all this data has bounded derivatives on each packet. At the initial radial split the barriers are the separate relatively compact domains in the radial pole bands, and the choices are locally finite with locally prescribed bounds.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cut-bv-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Every smooth positive weight and smooth locally bounded pressure on a smooth manifold admit a local absolute minimizer. Suppose on exhausting coordinate windows that metrics and positive weights converge smoothly and pressures converge locally uniformly. Any sequence of corresponding local absolute minimizers has a subsequence whose indicators converge in $L^1_{\\rm loc}$ to a local absolute minimizer of the limit data. Along that subsequence the weighted perimeter measures converge weakly locally, as do the unweighted perimeter measures and their associated unoriented perimeter varifolds. The assertion includes smooth pointed rescalings when the rescaled data have the stated convergence.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cut-gmt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ satisfy <a href=\"#cut:local-minimality\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cut:local-minimality\">[cut:local-minimality]</a> on a smooth $m$-manifold, $m\\ge2$, with smooth positive $\\omega$ and smooth $p$. Put $S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}|D\\chi_A|$. Then $S=S_{\\rm reg}\\cup\\Sigma$, where $S_{\\rm reg}$ is a smooth embedded hypersurface, $\\Sigma$ is relatively closed, and $$\\label{cut:singular-dimension}\n                         \\dim_{\\mathcal H}\\Sigma\\le m-8.$$ A negative dimension bound means that $\\Sigma$ is empty. The outward measure-theoretic normal extends smoothly on $S_{\\rm reg}$. With $B(u,v)=\\langle\\nabla_u\\nu,v\\rangle$, its base mean curvature satisfies $$\\label{cut:base-mean-curvature}\n                    H_g=p-\\partial_\\nu\\log\\omega.$$ The unweighted multiplicity-one perimeter varifold has locally bounded generalized mean curvature. Its density exists at every support point; monotonicity gives local uniform area upper bounds and positive area lower bounds at support points. Under the convergences of <a href=\"#cut:bv-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cut:bv-compactness\">39</a>, densities are upper semicontinuous at converging points, provided the local generalized-mean-curvature bounds are uniform. The supports converge locally, and on every compact subset of the regular limiting hypersurface convergence is smooth and single-sheeted when the data converge smoothly. On compact subsets of the interiors of the limiting phases there is eventually no boundary.*\n\n*Every tangent at a point of $S$ is a multiplicity-one locally perimeter-minimizing boundary cone in the Euclidean tangent space. A flat tangent implies regularity. A tangent at a singular point is therefore nonflat, and its translation-invariance spine has dimension at most $m-8$.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cut-gmt",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ satisfy <a href=\"#cut:local-minimality\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cut:local-minimality\">[cut:local-minimality]</a> on a smooth $m$-manifold, $m\\ge2$, with smooth positive $\\omega$ and smooth $p$. Put $S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}|D\\chi_A|$. Then $S=S_{\\rm reg}\\cup\\Sigma$, where $S_{\\rm reg}$ is a smooth embedded hypersurface, $\\Sigma$ is relatively closed, and $$\\label{cut:singular-dimension}\n                         \\dim_{\\mathcal H}\\Sigma\\le m-8.$$ A negative dimension bound means that $\\Sigma$ is empty. The outward measure-theoretic normal extends smoothly on $S_{\\rm reg}$. With $B(u,v)=\\langle\\nabla_u\\nu,v\\rangle$, its base mean curvature satisfies $$\\label{cut:base-mean-curvature}\n                    H_g=p-\\partial_\\nu\\log\\omega.$$ The unweighted multiplicity-one perimeter varifold has locally bounded generalized mean curvature. Its density exists at every support point; monotonicity gives local uniform area upper bounds and positive area lower bounds at support points. Under the convergences of <a href=\"#cut:bv-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cut:bv-compactness\">39</a>, densities are upper semicontinuous at converging points, provided the local generalized-mean-curvature bounds are uniform. The supports converge locally, and on every compact subset of the regular limiting hypersurface convergence is smooth and single-sheeted when the data converge smoothly. On compact subsets of the interiors of the limiting phases there is eventually no boundary.*\n\n*Every tangent at a point of $S$ is a multiplicity-one locally perimeter-minimizing boundary cone in the Euclidean tangent space. A flat tangent implies regularity. A tangent at a singular point is therefore nonflat, and its translation-invariance spine has dimension at most $m-8$.",

        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A$ satisfy <a href=\"#cut:local-minimality\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cut:local-minimality\">[cut:local-minimality]</a> on a smooth $m$-manifold, $m\\ge2$, with smooth positive $\\omega$ and smooth $p$. Put $S=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}|D\\chi_A|$. Then $S=S_{\\rm reg}\\cup\\Sigma$, where $S_{\\rm reg}$ is a smooth embedded hypersurface, $\\Sigma$ is relatively closed, and $$\\label{cut:singular-dimension}\n                         \\dim_{\\mathcal H}\\Sigma\\le m-8.$$ A negative dimension bound means that $\\Sigma$ is empty. The outward measure-theoretic normal extends smoothly on $S_{\\rm reg}$. With $B(u,v)=\\langle\\nabla_u\\nu,v\\rangle$, its base mean curvature satisfies $$\\label{cut:base-mean-curvature}\n                    H_g=p-\\partial_\\nu\\log\\omega.$$ The unweighted multiplicity-one perimeter varifold has locally bounded generalized mean curvature. Its density exists at every support point; monotonicity gives local uniform area upper bounds and positive area lower bounds at support points. Under the convergences of <a href=\"#cut:bv-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cut:bv-compactness\">39</a>, densities are upper semicontinuous at converging points, provided the local generalized-mean-curvature bounds are uniform. The supports converge locally, and on every compact subset of the regular limiting hypersurface convergence is smooth and single-sheeted when the data converge smoothly. On compact subsets of the interiors of the limiting phases there is eventually no boundary.*\n\n*Every tangent at a point of $S$ is a multiplicity-one locally perimeter-minimizing boundary cone in the Euclidean tangent space. A flat tangent implies regularity. A tangent at a singular point is therefore nonflat, and its translation-invariance spine has dimension at most $m-8$.",
      "explanation": "Minimizer boundaries are smooth off a closed singular set of codimension at least eight.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "source_id": "PM-336.3"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cut-end-convergence",
        "exact_quote": "For the common generic shift, let $A_E$ be the true phase on each packet and let $A_B$ be that on a face. On translated windows escaping in a product collar over a compact part of $B$, the phases $A_E$ converge to $A_B\\times\\mathbb R^d$ in $L^1_{\\rm loc}$, their perimeter measures and varifolds converge, and their regular boundaries converge smoothly on compact sets off the limiting singular set. Their boundary supports converge locally as well. The same conclusions hold after passage to any deeper product furnished by <a href=\"#packet:escape\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"packet:escape\">33</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

Their boundary supports converge locally as well. The same conclusions hold after passage to any deeper product furnished by <a href=\"#packet:escape\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"packet:escape\">33</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cut-side-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "Every nonnegative compactly supported smooth function $f$ on the closed regular side, with support avoiding the singular set, satisfies $$\\label{cut:deletion}\n       \\int_S f\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_S\n       \\le\\int_\\Omega\\bigl(|\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}f|+p_sf\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu.$$ For every smooth real test $v$ of the same type, $$\\label{cut:side-form}\n 3.05\\int_\\Omega|\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}v|^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\n       +2\\int_S H_{\\rm out}v^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_S\n       \\ge-1.02\\int_\\Omega\\Pi_{j_0}v^2\\,\\mathrm d\\mu.$$ Both statements extend to compactly supported Lipschitz tests by approximation.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-anchors",
        "exact_quote": "There are compatible smooth functions $h_j$, for the remaining labels, within $.1$ of $\\max(-1,\\min(1,\\mu_j))$, and common numbers $t_j\\in(-.3,.3)$ such that, on the singular set in each parent of dimension $m$, at most $m-8$ of the equalities $h_j=t_j$ hold. The assertion is vacuous in dimensions below eight. Moreover $$\\{h_j>t_j\\}\\cap\\{\\mu_j=-\\infty\\}=\\varnothing,\n \\qquad\n \\{h_j<t_j\\}\\cap\\{\\mu_j=+\\infty\\}=\\varnothing.$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-local-flat-pieces",
        "exact_quote": "Let $O$ be an open subset of a parent packet and let $\\eta>0$.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-local-shell",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a compact subset of $\\Sigma\\cap\\{h_j>t_j\\}$. For every sufficiently small $\\varepsilon>0$ there is an extended-real profile $u_\\varepsilon$, equal to $+\\infty$ for $\\delta\\le\\varepsilon$ and $-\\infty$ for $\\delta\\ge R_\\varepsilon$, where $$\\delta(x)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,F),\\qquad R_\\varepsilon=e^{5\\pi}\\varepsilon,$$ whose finite truncations are locally Lipschitz and which satisfies $$\\label{local:shell-gradient}\n 2|\\nabla u_\\varepsilon|\n       \\le .58u_\\varepsilon^2+2\\delta^{-2}\n       \\quad (\\varepsilon<\\delta<R_\\varepsilon).$$ There is a smooth nonnegative ambient function $D_\\varepsilon$ with $$\\label{local:shell-potential}\n \\begin{aligned}\n D_\\varepsilon&\\ge2\\delta^{-2}\n       &&\\text{on }\\{\\varepsilon\\le\\delta\\le R_\\varepsilon\\},\\\\\n D_\\varepsilon&\\le2.12\\delta^{-2}\n       &&\\text{everywhere off }F,\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}D_\\varepsilon&\\subset\n       \\{\\varepsilon/2<\\delta<2R_\\varepsilon\\}\n       \\Subset\\{h_j>t_j\\}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Replacing $\\mu_j$ by $\\max(\\mu_j,u_\\varepsilon)$, adding $D_\\varepsilon$ to $\\Pi_j$, and adding $\\{\\delta<\\varepsilon/2\\}$ to $U_j^+$ preserves the profile inequality with coefficient $a_r$. There is an identical negative construction in $\\{h_j<t_j\\}$, using $\\min(\\mu_j,-u_\\varepsilon)$ and enlarging $U_j^-$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-rough",
        "exact_quote": "For each $m\\ge8$ there are numbers $\\eta_0>0$ and $c_0>0$ with the following property. Suppose $F$ satisfies <a href=\"#local:flatness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"local:flatness\">[local:flatness]</a> with $\\eta\\le\\eta_0$ in a sufficiently accurate buffered chart. There are $d_F>0$ and $C_F<\\infty$ such that, on the wall and on either joined side, $$\\label{local:rough-estimate}\n c_0\\int_{\\delta<d_F}\\delta^{-2}v^2\n             \\le\\mathcal N(v)+C_F\\|v\\|_2^2.$$ The constants $\\eta_0,c_0$ depend only on $m$ and a fixed permitted chart distortion. The constants $d_F,C_F$ may depend on the compact piece, its scale threshold, and the local smooth metric, weight, and pressure. No principal-curvature bound for the wall is assumed.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-local-sharp",
        "exact_quote": "There is a sufficiently small flatness number $\\eta>0$, depending only on $m$ and the fixed numerical coefficients, with the following property. Let $F$ be any piece from <a href=\"#local:flat-pieces\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"local:flat-pieces\">49</a> for this flatness number, in a sufficiently accurate chart. For the potential of <a href=\"#local:shell\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"local:shell\">50</a>, there are $\\varepsilon_F>0$ and $C_F<\\infty$ such that, for every $0<\\varepsilon<\\varepsilon_F$, $$\\label{local:sharp-estimate}\n 1.16\\int D_\\varepsilon v^2\n           \\le .78\\mathcal N(v)+C_F\\|v\\|_2^2.$$ This holds on the true wall and either joined side, and hence on translated compact tests in a pure wall cylinder. The constants $\\varepsilon_F,C_F$ may depend on the local smooth data and $F$, but are independent of $\\varepsilon$. No vanishing trace condition is imposed at a joined seam.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shield-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $m\\ge8$. On any fixed compact window, a bounded sequence in $\\mathcal H_Z$ has a strongly convergent $L^2$ subsequence. The following moving version also holds. Work in fixed smooth ambient geometry on each expanding window, as is the case after translation in an exact product end. Suppose positive weights and pressures converge smoothly there, and the true phases and walls converge as in [cut:gmt,cut:end-convergence] [cut:gmt](#cut:gmt), [cut:end-convergence](#cut:end-convergence). Consider normalized tests on the corresponding raw pieces $Z_i$, all supported in a fixed compact window strictly inside a larger window of convergence. After passage to a subsequence there is a function $v$ on the limiting raw piece $Z_\\infty$ such that:*\n\n1.  *On every regular compact subwindow, the tests converge weakly in $H^1$ and strongly in $L^2$ in the smooth graphical identifications. At a limiting regular seam the two traces agree.*\n\n2.  *No $L^2$ mass is lost in neighborhoods of the limiting singular set. In particular, integrals of $v_i^2$ converge to those of $v^2$, including integrals against any fixed bounded continuous ambient potential and its pullback to the wall and cylinder.*\n\n\n3.  *The limit belongs to $\\mathcal H_{Z_\\infty}$ and $$\\label{shield:liminf}\n     \\mathfrak h_{Z_\\infty}(v)\n           \\le\\liminf_i\\mathfrak h_{Z_i}(v_i).$$*\n\n*These assertions include pure cylinders, translated cylinder intervals, and Euclidean product limits. Constants are local to the enlarged window; no uniform bound among all packets is asserted. If the limiting wall is smooth on the enlarged window, the same conclusions follow from ordinary Rellich compactness and the bounded trace maps in the smooth graphical charts, in every base dimension.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },

Let $F\\subset\\Sigma$ be a compact piece and let $A_\\varepsilon=1.16D_\\varepsilon$ be one of the nonnegative shell potentials of [local:shell,local:sharp] [local:shell](#local:shell), [local:sharp](#local:sharp). In particular its base support lies in a fixed compact set and shrinks to $F$, and $$\\label{shield:local-input}\n \\int_Z A_\\varepsilon v^2\\le .78\\mathcal N_Z(v)+C_F\\norm v_2^2$$ for all sufficiently small $\\varepsilon$, on all three pieces.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shield-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a packet, let $D\\ge0$ be a fixed smooth compactly supported potential addition on $B$, and let $V$ denote the old cumulative potential on every packet. Suppose these old potentials are exactly compatible and obey the common form bound $\\theta\\mathfrak h$. Suppose also that $$\\int(V_B+1.16D)v^2\\le\\beta\\mathfrak h_B(v),\\qquad \\beta\\ge\\theta,$$ on the wall and joined sides over $B$. In each of the finitely many coface occurrences $B\\times(T,\\infty)^d\\subset E$, introduce a smooth potential $D_E$ with $$\\label{shield:extension-support}\n 0\\le D_E\\le D\\circ\\pi_B,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}D_E\\subset K\\times\\prod_{i=1}^d[R_i,\\infty),\n \\qquad K\\Subset B.$$ Use consistent functions on the normal coordinates identified by commuting face chains, so that these extensions are compatible. For every $\\eta>0$ all starts can be chosen sufficiently large that $$\\label{shield:extended-bound}\n \\int(V_E+1.16D_E)v^2\\le(\\beta+\\eta)\\mathfrak h_E(v)$$ on every affected raw piece. The conclusion concerns the same fixed true cuts; it uses their product convergence from <a href=\"#cut:end-convergence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cut:end-convergence\">43</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-shield-global",
        "exact_quote": "At a fixed stage $r$, let $j_0$ be the current label and keep its true minimizers fixed. There are updates of every remaining profile and core, and compatible smooth additions $D_j\\ge0$, with the following properties on every parent packet $E$ of dimension $m$:*\n\n\n1.  *The updated profiles have locally Lipschitz finite truncations and satisfy, almost everywhere on their finite bands, $$\\label{shield:profile-output}\n     2\\abs{\\nabla\\mu'_j}\\le a_r(\\mu'_j)^2+\\Pi_j+D_j.$$ All these data are exact compatible products. Each core only enlarges, and its closure remains in the interior of its designated updated pole set. The original forced sets and the anchor sign restrictions are preserved.*\n\n2.  *With $G'_j=E\\setminus(U_j^{\\prime+}\\cup U_j^{\\prime-})$, the set $$\\label{shield:guard-output}\n     H_E^*=\\left\\{x:\\#\\{j\\ne j_0:x\\in G'_j\\}\\ge m-2\\right\\}$$ is closed and misses $\\Sigma_E$. In an exact collar over a face $B$, its projection is contained in $H_B^*$. This holds for every further face and every simultaneous product limit.*\n\n\n3.  *For one common $\\theta_*<.9$, all walls and joined sides satisfy $$\\label{shield:form-output}\n     \\int 1.16\\sum_{j\\ne j_0}D_jv^2\n     \\le\\theta_*\\bigl(\\mathcal N(v)+e_0\\norm v_2^2\\bigr)\n     \\le .9\\bigl(\\mathcal N(v)+e_0\\norm v_2^2\\bigr).$$ The additions are bounded with their derivatives on each post-radial packet. There are only finitely many local additions on each such packet and only finitely many extensions into it.*\n\n*For the initial radial parent the same conclusions hold with locally finite additions over its compact transition shells; boundedness is asserted on the corresponding compact blocks and <a href=\"#shield:form-output\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"shield:form-output\">[shield:form-output]</a> holds on every compact test. No convergence assertion at an end of the original manifold is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-shield-global",
          "exact_quote": "At a fixed stage $r$, let $j_0$ be the current label and keep its true minimizers fixed. There are updates of every remaining profile and core, and compatible smooth additions $D_j\\ge0$, with the following properties on every parent packet $E$ of dimension $m$:*\n\n\n1.  *The updated profiles have locally Lipschitz finite truncations and satisfy, almost everywhere on their finite bands, $$\\label{shield:profile-output}\n     2\\abs{\\nabla\\mu'_j}\\le a_r(\\mu'_j)^2+\\Pi_j+D_j.$$ All these data are exact compatible products. Each core only enlarges, and its closure remains in the interior of its designated updated pole set. The original forced sets and the anchor sign restrictions are preserved.*\n\n2.  *With $G'_j=E\\setminus(U_j^{\\prime+}\\cup U_j^{\\prime-})$, the set $$\\label{shield:guard-output}\n     H_E^*=\\left\\{x:\\#\\{j\\ne j_0:x\\in G'_j\\}\\ge m-2\\right\\}$$ is closed and misses $\\Sigma_E$. In an exact collar over a face $B$, its projection is contained in $H_B^*$. This holds for every further face and every simultaneous product limit.*\n\n\n3.  *For one common $\\theta_*<.9$, all walls and joined sides satisfy $$\\label{shield:form-output}\n     \\int 1.16\\sum_{j\\ne j_0}D_jv^2\n     \\le\\theta_*\\bigl(\\mathcal N(v)+e_0\\norm v_2^2\\bigr)\n     \\le .9\\bigl(\\mathcal N(v)+e_0\\norm v_2^2\\bigr).$$ The additions are bounded with their derivatives on each post-radial packet. There are only finitely many local additions on each such packet and only finitely many extensions into it.*\n\n*For the initial radial parent the same conclusions hold with locally finite additions over its compact transition shells; boundedness is asserted on the corresponding compact blocks and <a href=\"#shield:form-output\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"shield:form-output\">[shield:form-output]</a> holds on every compact test. No convergence assertion at an end of the original manifold is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-336.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At a fixed stage $r$, let $j_0$ be the current label and keep its true minimizers fixed. There are updates of every remaining profile and core, and compatible smooth additions $D_j\\ge0$, with the following properties on every parent packet $E$ of dimension $m$:*\n\n\n1.  *The updated profiles have locally Lipschitz finite truncations and satisfy, almost everywhere on their finite bands, $$\\label{shield:profile-output}\n     2\\abs{\\nabla\\mu'_j}\\le a_r(\\mu'_j)^2+\\Pi_j+D_j.$$ All these data are exact compatible products. Each core only enlarges, and its closure remains in the interior of its designated updated pole set. The original forced sets and the anchor sign restrictions are preserved.*\n\n2.  *With $G'_j=E\\setminus(U_j^{\\prime+}\\cup U_j^{\\prime-})$, the set $$\\label{shield:guard-output}\n     H_E^*=\\left\\{x:\\#\\{j\\ne j_0:x\\in G'_j\\}\\ge m-2\\right\\}$$ is closed and misses $\\Sigma_E$. In an exact collar over a face $B$, its projection is contained in $H_B^*$. This holds for every further face and every simultaneous product limit.*\n\n\n3.  *For one common $\\theta_*<.9$, all walls and joined sides satisfy $$\\label{shield:form-output}\n     \\int 1.16\\sum_{j\\ne j_0}D_jv^2\n     \\le\\theta_*\\bigl(\\mathcal N(v)+e_0\\norm v_2^2\\bigr)\n     \\le .9\\bigl(\\mathcal N(v)+e_0\\norm v_2^2\\bigr).$$ The additions are bounded with their derivatives on each post-radial packet. There are only finitely many local additions on each such packet and only finitely many extensions into it.*\n\n*For the initial radial parent the same conclusions hold with locally finite additions over its compact transition shells; boundedness is asserted on the corresponding compact blocks and <a href=\"#shield:form-output\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"shield:form-output\">[shield:form-output]</a> holds on every compact test. No convergence assertion at an end of the original manifold is needed.",

        "anchor": "stmt-regular-guards",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite integer $J\\ge4$. On each parent packet $E$, let $\\varsigma_E$ be signed distance to the true boundary support, truncated at $\\pm1$, positive in the plus phase. If the support is empty on a component, use the corresponding constant sign. After the radial split, the following choices can be made on each whole parent packet.*\n\n1.  *There is a positive $\\rho_E<1/10$ such that near $$\\{x:\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,H_E^*)<4\\rho_E,\\ |\\varsigma_E(x)|<4\\rho_E\\}$$ the true boundary is regular, has an embedded normal tube, and signed distance has bounded derivatives through order $J+2$ on a slightly smaller tube. The current forced core closures have positive uniform distance from the true boundary.*\n\n2.  *On an elementary collar $B\\times(T,\\infty)$, signed distances converge uniformly to the pullback of $\\varsigma_B$ as $T\\to\\infty$. They converge in $C^J$ on the guarded tubes, with the coface tube chosen inside the face’s tube. These statements are uniform over the whole face, including its noncompact ends.*\n\n*For the initial radial parent, assertion <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> holds separately on each enlarged compact radial block, with its own radius and derivative bounds; assertion <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> is not required. The radii depend on the true partitions and the updated guards, and can be chosen before any smooth-approximation tolerances.",
        "source_id": "PM-336.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-regular-partition",
        "exact_quote": "There are compatible smooth defining functions $g_E$ with $0$ a regular value, positive and negative on the respective current forced core closures, and with $L_E=g_E^{-1}(0)\\subset\\operatorname{int}G_{j_0}$. After the radial split they can be chosen uniformly as close as prescribed to $\\varsigma_E$ in value and in $C^J$ on smaller guarded tubes. In a buffered neighborhood of $H_E^*$, a smooth diffeomorphism arbitrarily close to the identity in $C^{J-1}$ identifies the smooth partition with the true partition. It uses the same map on the wall and on the boundaries of its two sides. At the initial radial step, the approximation tolerances and comparison buffers are chosen separately on enlarged compact radial blocks. The defining functions, but not this comparison map, are exact products in sufficiently far old collars.",

        "exact_quote": "A continuous scalar function on a packet can be approximated by smooth functions, with any prescribed positive value error, by operators that reproduce constants.         "anchor": "stmt-regular-correction",
        "exact_quote": "There are smooth nonnegative bounded compatible potentials $K_A$, with all derivatives bounded packetwise, such that $K_A=0$ in a positive uniform neighborhood of $D_A$ and $$\\label{regular:global-gap}\n Q_A(u):=\\int\\bigl(C|\\nabla u|^2+\n       (R_{\\rm old}-V_A^*+K_A)u^2\\bigr)\n \\ge g_A\\|u\\|_2^2\\qquad(g_A>0)$$ for every compact smooth test. Let $\\omega_A^{\\rm old}$ be the product of the retained circle length functions for the smoothed metric on $A$, before the new circle factors at this split are appended. With $K_A$ acting by multiplication, define $$L_A=L_A^0+K_A\n     =-C\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\Delta}}_{\\rm old}+R_{\\rm old}-V_A^*+K_A.$$ Its quadratic form in the measure $\\omega_A^{\\rm old}\\,\\mathrm d\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_A$ is $Q_A$. In an exact end over $B$, the base metric is a product and every retained warp pulls back from $B$. Consequently $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\Delta}}_{{\\rm old},A}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\Delta}}_{{\\rm old},B}+\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\Delta}}_{\\rm normal},\n \\qquad L_A=L_B-C\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\Delta}}_{\\rm normal}.$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Urysohn d-width** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a metric space $X$ and an integer $d\ge0$, define its *Urysohn $d$-width* by $$\mathop{\mathrm{UW}}_d(X)=\inf_f\ \sup_{y\in K}\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}_X f^{-1}(y),$$ where the infimum runs over continuous maps $f:X\to K$ to simplicial complexes of dimension at most $d$.
- **continuous macroscopic dimension** ([source](#sec:introduction)): $$ ◻ </details> For the closed-cover consequence we also use the *continuous macroscopic dimension*: $\dim_{\mathrm{mc}}X\le d$ means that there is a continuous map to a simplicial complex of dimension at most $d$, with its uniform simplex metric and a finite uniform bound on the diameters of its entire fibers.
- **sign conventions** ([source](#stmt-conv-signs)): The Laplacian is $\Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}$, and round spheres have positive scalar curvature. For a hypersurface with unit normal $\nu$, its second fundamental form is $B(v,w)=\langle\nabla_v\nu,w\rangle$, and its mean curvature is $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}B$. Integration and divergence use Riemannian densities, so no orientation is needed. Distances in the final fiber bound are always measured in the original metric $g$.
- **packet diagram** ([source](#stmt-packet-category)): At stage $r$, a status is $\alpha\in\{+,-,*\}^{r}$. Its entries record the first $r$ processed cuts. A sign $+$ or $-$ selects the corresponding retained side of a cut, and a star $*$ selects the wall, called a *hit*. The space $E_\alpha$ is a smooth second countable manifold without boundary, possibly empty or disconnected; when nonempty its base dimension is $m(\alpha)=n-\#\{i:\alpha_i=*\}$. Its connected components are called *packets*. Replacing signs by stars gives the face order. If $B$ is a packet with $d$ additional stars relative to a coarser status, then for that coarser status it has exactly one incident packet $E$ and one corresponding occurrence of a full product tail $$\label{packet:tail} \iota_{BE}:B\times(T_1,\infty)\times\cdots\times(T_d,\infty) \longrightarrow E.$$ The normal coordinates are labelled by the changed status entries. Thresholds may depend on the incidence. For a single entry, tails from distinct face components are disjoint. Intersections of sufficiently far tails for different entries are exactly the tails over their common further faces. The identifications commute: composing face maps in any order gives the same occurrence and the same individually labelled normal coordinates. After slightly raising the entry thresholds, each closed tail is properly embedded. Each packet $E$ is equipped with a continuous map $q_E:E\to X$. After the radial stage we require the following additional properties. Every packet has only finitely many incident faces and cofaces, including through chains; the complement of its sufficiently far elementary tails is compact. On each sufficiently far face product these maps satisfy $q_E(\iota_{BE}(b,\mathbf t))=q_B(b)$. A metric, scalar function, warp, or set is *compatible* if, in every sufficiently far tail, it is exactly the product or pullback of the corresponding face datum. For metrics the normal factors are $\mathrm dt_1^2+\cdots+\mathrm dt_d^2$; all warps are independent of the normal coordinates there. The thresholds for different data may differ.
- **analytic invariant** ([source](#stmt-packet-invariant)): At stage $r$, write $J_r$ for the unprocessed labels. On a packet $E$ of base dimension $m$, retain its base metric and all circle warps. Write $R_E$ for the scalar curvature of that full warped metric and $\omega_E$ for the product of its circle lengths, suppressing fixed circle-volume constants. Gradients of invariant functions are base gradients; their integrals use $\omega_E\,\mathrm d\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_E$, and their Laplacian is the corresponding weighted base Laplacian. For every $j\in J_r$ the data comprise a compatible continuous profile $\mu_j:E\to[-\infty,\infty]$ with locally Lipschitz finite truncations, a compatible smooth potential $\Pi_j\ge\pi_0$, and compatible open cores $U_j^+,U_j^-$ satisfying $$\label{packet:core-poles} \overline{U_j^\pm}\subset \operatorname{int}_E\{\mu_j=\pm\infty\}.$$ On the open finite band the profile obeys, almost everywhere, $$\label{packet:profile} 2\|\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}\mu_j\|\le a_r\mu_j^2+\Pi_j.$$ Set $$\label{packet:required-set} G_j=E\setminus(U_j^+\cup U_j^-),\qquad D_E=\bigl\{x:\#\{j\in J_r:x\in G_j\}\ge m-1\bigr\}.$$ The required scalar bound is $$\label{packet:curvature} R_E\ge\lambda_r+b_r\sum_{j\in J_r}\Pi_j \quad\hbox{on }D_E.$$ For an unprocessed color $i$, the cores contain $q_E^{-1}(A_i)\subset U_i^+$ and $q_E^{-1}(B_i)\subset U_i^-$. Enlarging cores must preserve these conditions. The sets $D_E$ are closed: they are finite unions of finite intersections of the closed sets $G_j$ (and equal $E$ when the count threshold is nonpositive). All data are exact products in the sense of <a href="#packet:category" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="packet:category">16</a>; at stage zero the single space is $X$, with no product-end requirement.
- **finite exceptional data** ([source](#stmt-countable-finite-data)): At stage $r\ge1$, on each whole packet $E$ there are finite sets $I_E\subset J_r$ of label indices and $F_E$ of old warp indices such that, for $j\notin I_E$, $$\label{countable:passive} \mu_j\equiv-\infty,\qquad U_j^-=E,\qquad U_j^+=\varnothing, \qquad \Pi_j\equiv c_rp_j^*.$$ For every old warp index outside $F_E$, its length is identically one. The number $c_r$ is the same on all packets and is prescribed by $$\label{countable:passive-multiplier} c_0=1,\qquad c_{r+1}=(1+3\eta_r/2)c_r.$$ For any finite diagram used in a comparison, take the union of its finite exceptional sets, adding the current label explicitly. This one finite union then works simultaneously on all its whole packets. At stage zero the analogous property is required on every compact radial block and buffer, with $c_0=1$.
- **local absolute minimizer** ([source](#stmt-cut-minimizer-definition)): For smooth $p$ a phase $A$ is a local absolute minimizer if for every relatively compact open $U\subset E$ and every locally finite-perimeter set $B$ with $A\mathbin\triangle B\Subset U$, $$\label{cut:local-minimality} P_\omega(A;U)-\int_{A\cap U}p\,\mathrm d\mu \le P_\omega(B;U)-\int_{B\cap U}p\,\mathrm d\mu.$$ The notation $A\mathbin\triangle B\Subset U$ means that the discrepancy is contained, modulo null sets, in a compact subset of $U$.
- **reserved form** ([source](#stmt-cut-reserve)): For the wall or a joined side define, respectively, $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal N_S(v)&=1.7\int_S\|\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}v\|^2\,\mathrm d\mu_S,\\ \mathcal N_\Omega(v) &=.8\int_\Omega\|\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}v_b\|^2\,\mathrm d\mu +1.7\int_0^\infty\!\int_S\|\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}v_c\|^2 \,\mathrm d\mu_S\,\mathrm dt. \end{aligned}$$ We write $\mathcal N$ when the tested space is understood. The squared norm $\\|v\\|_2^2$ is the sum of the corresponding piecewise weighted integrals. With $$e_0=(\lambda_r-\lambda_{r+1})/100>0, \qquad \mathfrak h(v)=\mathcal N(v)+e_0\\|v\\|_2^2,$$ the test completion means completion of the admissible compact tests just specified in the norm $\mathfrak h^{1/2}$. In particular its seam condition is matching traces, with no zero trace imposed there.
- **matching form domain** ([source](#stmt-shield-domain)): Let $E$ be a parent packet of dimension $m$, with true wall $S$ and singular set $\Sigma$. A raw piece $Z$ means either $S_{\rm reg}$ or one of the two joined spaces $$Z=\Omega\mathbin{\cup}_{S_{\rm reg}} (S_{\rm reg}\times[0,\infty)).$$ The tests on a joined space are compactly supported, piecewise smooth functions whose two traces agree at the regular seam and whose support avoids $\Sigma$ and the cylinders over $\Sigma$. The wall has the corresponding compact tests away from $\Sigma$. All measures include the old warp weight. Put $$\label{shield:hilbert} \mathfrak h_Z(v)=\mathcal N_Z(v)+e_0\norm{v}_{L^2(Z)}^2, \qquad e_0=\frac{\lambda_r-\lambda_{r+1}}{100}>0,$$ where $\mathcal N$ is the reserved gradient form in <a href="#cut:reserve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cut:reserve">47</a>: its gradient coefficients are $.8$ on the bulk and $1.7$ on the wall or cylinder. Write $\mathcal H_Z$ for the completion of these tests in $\mathfrak h_Z^{1/2}$. Pure cylinders $S_{\rm reg}\times\mathbb R$ use the coefficient $1.7$ throughout. The definitions also apply after adjoining finitely many Euclidean product coordinates, with the same coefficients and $e_0$. Such products are used only to identify limits on translated windows; they do not append further circle warps or change the weight. A compact window means a fixed compact ambient base window together with a bounded cylinder interval, when a cylinder is present.

The sets $D_E$ are closed: they are finite unions of finite intersections of the closed sets $G_j$ (and equal $E$ when the count threshold is nonpositive). All data are exact products in the sense of <a href=\"#packet:category\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"packet:category\">16</a>; at stage zero the single space is $X$, with no product-end requirement.",
      "interpretation": "Profiles, potentials, cores and a scalar bound required on the set of at least m-1 undecided labels.",
      "kind": "invariant",
      "name": "analytic invariant",
      "scope": "Stage r packets of base dimension m."
    },

          "exact_quote": "At stage $r\\ge1$, on each whole packet $E$ there are finite sets $I_E\\subset J_r$ of label indices and $F_E$ of old warp indices such that, for $j\\notin I_E$, $$\\label{countable:passive}\n \\mu_j\\equiv-\\infty,\\qquad U_j^-=E,\\qquad U_j^+=\\varnothing,\n \\qquad \\Pi_j\\equiv c_rp_j^*.$$ For every old warp index outside $F_E$, its length is identically one. The number $c_r$ is the same on all packets and is prescribed by $$\\label{countable:passive-multiplier}\n c_0=1,\\qquad c_{r+1}=(1+3\\eta_r/2)c_r.$$ For any finite diagram used in a comparison, take the union of its finite exceptional sets, adding the current label explicitly. This one finite union then works simultaneously on all its whole packets. At stage zero the analogous property is required on every compact radial block and buffer, with $c_0=1$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-cut-minimizer-definition",
          "exact_quote": "For smooth $p$ a phase $A$ is a local absolute minimizer if for every relatively compact open $U\\subset E$ and every locally finite-perimeter set $B$ with $A\\mathbin\\triangle B\\Subset U$, $$\\label{cut:local-minimality}\n P_\\omega(A;U)-\\int_{A\\cap U}p\\,\\mathrm d\\mu\n \\le P_\\omega(B;U)-\\int_{B\\cap U}p\\,\\mathrm d\\mu.$$ The notation $A\\mathbin\\triangle B\\Subset U$ means that the discrepancy is contained, modulo null sets, in a compact subset of $U$.",

A raw piece $Z$ means either $S_{\\rm reg}$ or one of the two joined spaces $$Z=\\Omega\\mathbin{\\cup}_{S_{\\rm reg}}\n                 (S_{\\rm reg}\\times[0,\\infty)).$$ The tests on a joined space are compactly supported, piecewise smooth functions whose two traces agree at the regular seam and whose support avoids $\\Sigma$ and the cylinders over $\\Sigma$. The wall has the corresponding compact tests away from $\\Sigma$. All measures include the old warp weight. Put $$\\label{shield:hilbert}\n \\mathfrak h_Z(v)=\\mathcal N_Z(v)+e_0\\norm{v}_{L^2(Z)}^2,\n \\qquad e_0=\\frac{\\lambda_r-\\lambda_{r+1}}{100}>0,$$ where $\\mathcal N$ is the reserved gradient form in <a href=\"#cut:reserve\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cut:reserve\">47</a>: its gradient coefficients are $.8$ on the bulk and $1.7$ on the wall or cylinder. Write $\\mathcal H_Z$ for the completion of these tests in $\\mathfrak h_Z^{1/2}$. Pure cylinders $S_{\\rm reg}\\times\\mathbb R$ use the coefficient $1.7$ throughout. The definitions also apply after adjoining finitely many Euclidean product coordinates, with the same coefficients and $e_0$. Such products are used only to identify limits on translated windows; they do not append further circle warps or change the weight. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-pressure-law** [claim](#stmt-pressure-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pressure-law): Prescribe the inverse in the first variable through a rounded maximum profile and invert by the implicit function theorem. -> pressure function
- **r-height-gradient** [claim](#stmt-step-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-step-existence): Barrier comparison for height, angle Jacobi identity for gradient, continuation in the parameter. -> smooth solutions with uniform bounds
- **r-locality** [claim](#stmt-step-locality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-step-locality): Exponential barrier comparison then rescaled Schauder bootstrap. -> decay on constant floor region
- **r-raw-scalar** [claim](#stmt-step-raw-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-step-raw-estimate): Foliation scalar formula and completion of squares in two slope regimes. -> raw drift inequality
- **r-local-stretching** [claim](#stmt-step-main) / [proof](#pm-h-12): Spatial cutoff of the raw output and curve-length argument for distance gain. -> stretched metric
- **r-round** [claim](#stmt-rounds-round) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rounds-round): Apply the stretching in order and telescope trace losses. -> complete round
- **r-warp-identity** [claim](#stmt-rounds-warp-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rounds-warp-identity): Koszul formula and sectional curvature summation. -> warped scalar formula
- **r-wide** [claim](#stmt-rounds-wide) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rounds-wide): Iterate rounds over growing scales and realize drift by many log warps. -> wide metric with warps
- **r-packet-split** [claim](#stmt-packet-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-packet-split): Clamp normal coordinates and cut along compatible regular functions. -> new packet diagram
- **r-packet-geometry** [claim](#stmt-packet-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-packet-geometry): Induct on faces using Lipschitz tail coordinates and sector decomposition. -> bounded geometry
- **r-packet-escape** [claim](#stmt-packet-escape) / [proof](#proof-stmt-packet-escape): Subsequence in a sector and product window. -> product windows
- **r-packet-approximation** [claim](#stmt-packet-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-packet-approximation): Allocate tolerances down chains then blend with weights. -> compatible smooth data
- **r-packet-reconstruction** [claim](#stmt-packet-reconstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-packet-reconstruction): Enlarge horizons, collar compression preserving strata. -> finite reconstruction
- **r-cut-pressure** [claim](#stmt-cut-pressure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-pressure): Clip and smooth the profile, choose barrier fields. -> smooth pressure
- **r-bv-compactness** [claim](#stmt-cut-bv-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-bv-compactness): Direct method on exhaustions, lattice inequality and slicing. -> minimizers and compactness
- **r-barriers** [claim](#stmt-cut-barriers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-barriers): Ball slicing differential inequality against volume growth. -> barrier containment
- **r-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-cut-generic-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-generic-uniqueness): Ordering of minimizers across shifts and countability of exceptional shifts. -> unique product minimizer
- **r-gmt** [claim](#stmt-cut-gmt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-gmt): First variation, blowup cones, Allard regularity, content argument. -> regular set and singular dimension
- **r-end-convergence** [claim](#stmt-cut-end-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-end-convergence): Compactness plus uniqueness identifies the limit. -> product limits
- **r-wall-stability** [claim](#stmt-cut-wall-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-wall-stability): Second variation and Gauss equation. -> wall form
- **r-side-estimate** [claim](#stmt-cut-side-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-side-estimate): Deletion comparison and Young inequality. -> seam estimate
- **r-raw-domination** [claim](#stmt-cut-raw-domination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-raw-domination): Add gradient surplus to the wall and side forms. -> raw form bounds
- **r-anchors** [claim](#stmt-local-anchors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-anchors): Measure-zero image of the singular set and Fubini over thresholds. -> anchors
- **r-flat-pieces** [claim](#stmt-local-flat-pieces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-flat-pieces): Tangent cone spine argument and countable decomposition. -> flat pieces
- **r-shell** [claim](#stmt-local-shell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-shell): Explicit Riccati crossing profile and annular potential. -> shell profile
- **r-rough-hardy** [claim](#stmt-local-rough) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-rough): Covering numbers, superharmonic potential, vector-field Hardy identity. -> rough Hardy bound
- **r-sharp-shielding** [claim](#stmt-local-sharp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-sharp): Localization, plane potentials, comparison with distance. -> local shielding bound
- **r-shield-compactness** [claim](#stmt-shield-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shield-compactness): Submanifold Sobolev, BV bound, capacity argument. -> window compactness
- **r-shield-maximum** [claim](#stmt-shield-maximum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shield-maximum): Localize then contradict by compactness. -> maximum rule
- **r-shield-extension** [claim](#stmt-shield-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shield-extension): Late fronts and product limit comparison. -> coface extension
- **r-shield-caps** [claim](#stmt-shield-caps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shield-caps): Minimum with product caps. -> compatible caps
- **r-shield-global** [claim](#stmt-shield-global) / [proof](#proof-stmt-shield-global): Finite termination by compactness of the risky set, summable allowances. -> shielded guard
- **r-regular-guards** [claim](#stmt-regular-guards) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-guards): Compactness and escape analysis for tube radii. -> tube control
- **r-regular-partition** [claim](#stmt-regular-partition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-partition): Smooth signed distance, blend, perturb to regular value, comparison diffeomorphism. -> smooth wall
- **r-regular-transfer** [claim](#stmt-regular-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-transfer): Count inclusions and numerical margins. -> transferred form
- **r-regular-seam** [claim](#stmt-regular-seam) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-seam): Variable-width concave smoothing. -> smooth metric
- **r-regular-constants** [claim](#stmt-regular-constant-preservation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-constant-preservation): Local averaging with partition of unity. -> constant-preserving smoothing
- **r-regular-correction** [claim](#stmt-regular-correction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-correction): Angle cutoff and finite partition of unity over collars. -> gapped operator
- **r-regular-resolvent** [claim](#stmt-regular-resolvent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-resolvent): Coercive form, Agmon weights, Schauder estimates. -> bounded solution
- **r-regular-solutions** [claim](#stmt-regular-solutions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-solutions): End decay then upward blending with preallocated errors. -> supersolution
- **r-regular-step** [claim](#stmt-regular-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-regular-step): Append warps by power of the supersolution and use the scalar identity. -> next-stage invariant
- **r-initialization** [claim](#stmt-packet-initialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-packet-small-displacement): Riccati crossing profile and proper radius function. -> stage zero data
- **r-finite-cut** [claim](#stmt-finite-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-finite-cut): Combine minimization, shielding and regularization. -> finite cutting
- **r-countable-initial** [claim](#stmt-countable-initial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-countable-initial): Crossing profiles and proper radius with plateau bands. -> countable initial data
- **r-countable-locality** [claim](#stmt-countable-locality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-countable-locality): Finite exceptional list and directed passes. -> finite dependency
- **r-countable-step** [claim](#stmt-countable-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-countable-step): Run pressure, shielding, regularization and warp addition with floors per label. -> stage-preserved invariant
- **r-dimension-one** [claim](#stmt-countable-dimension-one) / [proof](#proof-stmt-countable-dimension-one): Riccati inequality for arctangent of the warp derivative. -> no one-dimensional packet
- **r-countable-construction** [claim](#stmt-countable-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-countable-construction): Induction over labels with dimension obstruction. -> finite-stage partitions
- **r-topology-partitions** [claim](#stmt-topology-partitions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-topology-partitions): Locally stationary limit of collar compressions on one copy. -> collapse and walls
- **r-thickening** [claim](#stmt-topology-thicken) / [proof](#proof-stmt-topology-thicken): Metric star refinement. -> open wall neighborhoods
- **r-main-width-map** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Distance coordinates, staircase triangulation, entire fiber diameter bound. -> map to complex
- **r-uniform-simplex** [claim](#stmt-cover-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cover-uniform): Realize staircase simplices as standard simplices. -> uniform metric map
- **r-reeb** [claim](#stmt-cover-reeb) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cover-reeb): Critical values, gradient flow edges, attachment. -> Reeb graph
- **r-cover-main** [claim](#stmt-cover-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cover-reeb-2): Dimension split: main theorem, geodesic index form, Reeb graph. -> cover dimension bound
- **r-filling-radius** [claim](#stmt-cor-filling-radius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-filling-radius): Scale the theorem and use the width comparison. -> filling radius bound
- **r-aspherical** [claim](#stmt-cor-aspherical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-aspherical): Lift to the universal cover and apply Gromov's noncompact argument. -> obstruction

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-staircase-simplex**: Every admissible coordinate vector lies in a staircase simplex whose vertices are indicator vectors obtained by adding the fractional coordinates one at a time in order of decreasing size, so the target complex has dimension at most the bound on fractional coordinates.
- **inv-coordinate-recovery**: Two coordinate vectors with the same image in the converted complex agree in every coordinate, so the conversion never merges distinct coordinate data and fibers of the coordinate map are preserved.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **au-no-unit-coordinate**: Feeding the staircase construction a coordinate vector in which no coordinate equals one. -> The base vertex set is empty, so the vertices are not vertices of the complex and the barycentric decomposition is not a point of the complex.
- **au-too-many-fractional**: Applying the construction to vectors having more fractional coordinates than the allowed bound. -> The resulting simplex has dimension above the claimed bound, so the dimension conclusion fails.
- **au-coordinates-outside-unit-interval**: Using the triangulation on a coordinate family whose values leave the unit interval or whose supports are not locally finite. -> The staircase simplices do not cover such vectors, and continuity of the coordinate map into the complex is not justified.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The staircase triangulation of the cubical set is an explicit finite construction: for each admissible finitely supported coordinate vector it determines the chain of vertices and the barycentric weights by sorting the fractional coordinates. The analytic cutting machinery of the paper is not finite and is not encoded here.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin; first line integer k (bound on fractional coordinates); second line space separated rationals t_0 t_1 ... (finitely supported coordinate vector)
# PRECONDITIONS: every t_i in [0,1], at least one t_i equals 1, at most k values strictly between 0 and 1; otherwise REJECT is printed
# OUTPUTS: one line per vertex of the staircase simplex containing t: sorted indices of its ones, then ' : ', then its barycentric weight; or the word REJECT
# TIME: O(m log m) for m coordinates (sorting the fractional ones)
# SPACE: O(m)
# ARITHMETIC: exact rationals via fractions.Fraction, no floating point
# FAILURE: inadmissible vectors print REJECT and the program exits normally
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split('\n')
    k = int(data[0].strip())
    t = [Fraction(x) for x in data[1].split()]
    ok = all(0 <= x <= 1 for x in t) and any(x == 1 for x in t)
    frac = [i for i, x in enumerate(t) if 0 < x < 1]
    if not ok or len(frac) > k:
        print('REJECT')
        return
    base = [i for i, x in enumerate(t) if x == 1]
    frac.sort(key=lambda i: (-t[i], i))
    cur = set(base)
    pieces = []
    prev = Fraction(1)
    for a in frac:
        w = prev - t[a]
        if w > 0:
            pieces.append((sorted(cur), w))
        cur.add(a)
        prev = t[a]
    if prev > 0:
        pieces.append((sorted(cur), prev))
    for verts, w in pieces:
        print(' '.join(str(v) for v in verts) + ' : ' + str(w))


main()
```

Weights are positive and sum to 1, vertices form a strictly increasing chain of index sets containing every index whose coordinate is 1, and the weighted sum of vertex indicators equals the input vector.",

The analytic cutting machinery of the paper is not finite and is not encoded here.",

(finitely supported coordinate vector)\n# PRECONDITIONS: every t_i in [0,1], at least one t_i equals 1, at most k values strictly between 0 and 1; otherwise REJECT is printed\n# OUTPUTS: one line per vertex of the staircase simplex containing t: sorted indices of its ones, then ' : ', then its barycentric weight; or the word REJECT\n# TIME: O(m log m) for m coordinates (sorting the fractional ones)\n# SPACE: O(m)\n# ARITHMETIC: exact rationals via fractions.Fraction, no floating point\n# FAILURE: inadmissible vectors print REJECT and the program exits normally\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef main():\n    data = sys.stdin.read().split('\\n')\n    k = int(data[0].strip())\n    t = [Fraction(x) for x in data[1].split()]\n    ok = all(0 <= x <= 1 for x in t) and any(x == 1 for x in t)\n    frac = [i for i, x in enumerate(t) if 0 < x < 1]\n    if not ok or len(frac) > k:\n        print('REJECT')\n        return\n    base = [i for i, x in enumerate(t) if x == 1]\n    frac.sort(key=lambda i: (-t[i], i))\n    cur = set(base)\n    pieces = []\n    prev = Fraction(1)\n    for a in frac:\n        w = prev - t[a]\n        if w > 0:\n            pieces.append((sorted(cur), w))\n        cur.add(a)\n        prev = t[a]\n    if prev > 0:\n        pieces.append((sorted(cur), prev))\n    for verts, w in pieces:\n        print(' '.join(str(v) for v in verts) + ' : ' + str(w))\n\n\nmain()\n",

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence of a map and a stabilized wall family, but no executable procedure with finite inputs is given. The constructions need minimizing sets, PDE solutions and countably many stages, so no reference program can be derived from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are complete smooth Riemannian manifolds with analytic metric modifications and minimizing cones; nothing in the paper describes a discretized simulation, mesh or physics step. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Reeb-graph lemma is a classical-style construction for a proper Morse function and could inspire a topological analysis utility, but the paper proves it only as a mathematical lemma with no tool specification, input format or cost. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The successive processing of labels has an algorithmic flavour, but each step relies on existence of minimizers, regular-value choices and elliptic solutions with no stated finite procedure, so no complexity or pseudocode is justified. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The coordinate vectors are converted into a simplicial complex with a uniform simplex metric by a staircase triangulation of cubes, which is an explicit combinatorial encoding of locally finite coordinate data. Only the encoding idea is concrete; no storage layout or cost is given. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No kernel, dataflow or arithmetic pipeline appears. The only explicit function is an analytic pressure law with sign and derivative constraints, which is a proof device rather than a hardware primitive. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no adversary, hardness assumption or protocol. The universal-cover statement is a pure geometric bound on macroscopic dimension. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem concerns exact Riemannian manifolds with a pointwise curvature bound, not data distributions, learning or manifold hypotheses on samples, so no dimension-reduction guarantee for datasets follows. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Local absolute minimizers of weighted perimeter minus a pressure-volume term are variational objects, but they are used for existence and regularity of cuts, with no solver, convergence rate or discrete problem formulation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite incidence of faces and cofaces among packets is a topological bookkeeping condition, not a communication, consensus or fault-tolerance model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | stated | The principal value is mathematical: an obstruction to positive scalar curvature on aspherical manifolds. It is a theorem about existence of metrics and offers no engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dimension-at-least-four** (hypothesis): The codimension-two width bound with a dimensional constant is asserted only for manifolds of dimension at least four. The main theorem does not give the map to a complex of dimension at most n-2 in dimensions two or three. Blocked use: ['Applying the constant C_n of the main theorem to surfaces or three-manifolds', 'Deriving the universal-cover statement in dimensions two and three from the main theorem rather than from the separate geodesic and Reeb-graph arguments']
- **limit-pointwise-scalar-curvature** (hypothesis): Scalar curvature is the ordinary pointwise invariant. A fixed-scale macroscopic scalar-curvature lower bound is not enough, and the paper records manifolds where such a bound holds but the width is arbitrarily large. Blocked use: ['Replacing the pointwise bound Scal at least one by a ball-volume comparison at a fixed scale', 'Using the width bound for metric spaces that are only coarsely or macroscopically positively curved']
- **limit-complete-boundaryless-smooth** (hypothesis): The manifold must be connected, complete, smooth and without boundary, with the curvature lower bound holding everywhere. Completeness is used to keep the stretched metrics complete and to make closed balls compact. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to manifolds with boundary', 'Applying it to incomplete metrics or to metrics that are only piecewise smooth', 'Applying it where the scalar-curvature bound fails on a nonempty set']
- **limit-closed-filling-radius** (hypothesis): The filling-radius bound is stated for closed connected manifolds of dimension at least four with a positive scalar-curvature lower bound sigma squared. The bound scales as the dimensional constant over sigma. Blocked use: ['Bounding the filling radius of noncompact manifolds', 'Using the bound in dimensions below four', 'Using the bound without a uniform positive curvature lower bound']
- **limit-constant-not-optimized** (approximation): The constant is C_n=4D+2 with D chosen in terms of n before the analytic estimates. The paper states that the construction does not aim to optimize it, and no numerical value of D or of the width scale W is computed. Blocked use: ['Quoting a numerical value of C_n', 'Treating the constant as sharp or as the smallest possible width bound', 'Comparing the constant with other width constants as if it were optimized']
- **limit-continuous-not-lipschitz** (formal_scope): The map to the complex is continuous with bounded entire fibers. The continuous invariant and the version requiring a Lipschitz map to a uniformly metrized complex are different, and converting the simplex metric supplies no Lipschitz bound. Blocked use: ['Reading the result as a Lipschitz or coarse-Lipschitz map to the complex', 'Using the conclusion to bound Lipschitz-type macroscopic dimension']
- **limit-finite-floor-vs-countable** (formal_scope): The finite cutting theorem uses a common positive floor and equally spaced stage parameters, which are bookkeeping for a finite list and give no uniform lower bound for the countable construction. The countable case needs individual floors and finite exceptional data on each whole packet. Blocked use: ['Applying the finite cutting theorem directly to countably many labels', 'Assuming a common positive potential floor or a global finite list of warps in the countable construction']
- **limit-singular-minimizers-high-dimension** (edge_case): Minimizing cut boundaries are smooth only off a closed singular set of Hausdorff dimension at most m-8. In base dimension at least eight the singular set can be nonempty, so the cuts are not regular without the shielding and guarded regularization steps. Blocked use: ['Assuming a minimizing cut is a smooth hypersurface in base dimension at least eight', 'Skipping shielding of singular points before building the smooth split']
- **limit-one-dimensional-packet** (formal_scope): The exclusion of one-dimensional packets is a finite-stage statement about the invariant on a finite warped product. It concerns each finite stage and not an infinite-stage analytic metric, weight or torus. Blocked use: ['Using the obstruction on a limiting object with infinitely many warp factors', 'Concluding anything about packets that do not satisfy the stated invariant']
- **limit-nonconstructive-minimizers** (nonconstructive): Cuts are obtained as local absolute minimizers by the direct method with compactness. Existence is proved, and no finite procedure to compute the minimizer is given. Blocked use: ['Presenting the cutting step as an algorithm with a running time', 'Computing the walls of the final partition for a given metric from the paper alone']
- **limit-aspherical-closed-dimension** (hypothesis): The aspherical obstruction is stated for closed smooth aspherical manifolds of dimension at least four. Closedness is used so that the deck group acts cocompactly on the universal cover. Blocked use: ['Applying it to noncompact aspherical manifolds', 'Applying it to dimensions below four from this statement alone']
- **limit-empty-outer-complement** (edge_case): The reconstruction assumes every outer complement is nonempty. If one is empty, the diameter of the manifold is already bounded and a constant map suffices, so the cutting construction is not used. Blocked use: ['Running the cutting and coordinate construction when some outer complement is empty']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the quantitative continuous form of Gromov's scalar-curvature conjecture in every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 4. Every complete connected smooth boundaryless <i>n</i>-manifold with scalar curvature at least one admits a continuous map to a simplicial complex of dimension at most $`n-2`$ whose entire fibers have diameter bounded only in terms of <i>n</i>. We also obtain the continuous macroscopic-dimension conclusion for universal covers of closed manifolds with positive scalar curvature in every dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 2.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | For every integer $n\ge4$, there is a finite constant $C_n$ such that every connected complete smooth Riemannian $n$-manifold $(M,g)$, without boundary and with $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge1$, admits |
| [stmt-cor-filling-radius](#stmt-cor-filling-radius) | corollary | `cor:filling-radius` | 2 | Let $(M,g)$ be a closed connected smooth Riemannian $n$-manifold with $n\ge4$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge\sigma^2$ for some $\sigma>0$. |
| [stmt-cover-main](#stmt-cover-main) | corollary | `cover:main` | 3 | Let $(M,h)$ be a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension $n\ge2$ with positive scalar curvature. |
| [stmt-cor-aspherical](#stmt-cor-aspherical) | corollary | `cor:aspherical` | 4 | No closed smooth aspherical manifold of dimension $n\ge4$ admits a Riemannian metric of strictly positive scalar curvature.* |
| [stmt-conv-signs](#stmt-conv-signs) | convention | `conv:signs` | 5 | The Laplacian is $\Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}$, and round spheres have positive scalar curvature. |
| [stmt-pressure-law](#stmt-pressure-law) | theorem | `pressure:law` | 6 | For every $A_0,P_0,\varepsilon,b_0>0$ there exist $0<S_0\le1$, $T\ge1$, and a smooth function $$F:(-S_0,S_0)\times[T,\infty)\longrightarrow\mathbb R$$ which is odd in $x$, has $F_x>0$, and satisfies |
| [stmt-pressure-parameter-independence](#stmt-pressure-parameter-independence) | remark | `pressure:parameter-independence` | - | Remark 7*. When $A_0,P_0,\varepsilon,b_0$ are prescribed using only the dimension and fixed numerical constants, the same is true of $S_0,T$ and the entire pressure law. |
| [stmt-step-main](#stmt-step-main) | theorem | `step:main` | 8 | Fix $n\ge2$ and $0<\delta<1/10$, and put $L_*=1.004$. |
| [stmt-step-existence](#stmt-step-existence) | lemma | `step:existence` | 9 | For each fixed $a>0$ as above and every $0<r\le1/2$, [step:PDE] at $\tau=1$ has a smooth solution $u$ on $N$. |
| [stmt-step-locality](#stmt-step-locality) | lemma | `step:locality` | 10 | Let $u_r$ be the solutions from 9 at $\tau=1$. |
| [stmt-step-raw-estimate](#stmt-step-raw-estimate) | lemma | `step:raw-estimate` | 11 | On this original neighborhood, the raw output satisfies |
| [stmt-rounds-round](#stmt-rounds-round) | lemma | `rounds:round` | 12 | Suppose that $h_0$ is a complete metric, $t_0\ge T$ is smooth, and $$\mathcal D(h_0,t_0)\ge c+t_0^2$$ for a constant $c$. |
| [stmt-rounds-warp-identity](#stmt-rounds-warp-identity) | lemma | `rounds:warp-identity` | 13 | For smooth functions $p_1,\ldots,p_k$ on $(M,h)$, the metric $\widehat h=h+\sum_{\alpha=1}^k e^{2p_\alpha}\mathrm d\theta_\alpha^2$ on $M\times\mathbb T^k$ has scalar curvature |
| [stmt-rounds-wide](#stmt-rounds-wide) | theorem | `rounds:wide` | 14 | Let $(M,g)$ be complete, connected and smooth, with $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge1$. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A lower bound for scalar curvature is a local condition. Gromov’s macroscopic dimension conjecture predicts a global consequence: at a controlled scale, two dimensions can be collapsed. We prove the quantitative form of this assertion for arbitrary complete manifolds in dimensions at least four.

Throughout, scalar curvature means the ordinary pointwise Riemannian invariant. Fixed-scale macroscopic scalar curvature uses ball-volume comparison at a prescribed positive radius (Kumar and Sen 2026, Definitions 1.2–1.3) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[kumarSen2026Width](#ref-kumarSen2026Width)].

For a metric space $X$ and an integer $d\ge0$, define its *Urysohn $d$-width* by $$\mathop{\mathrm{UW}}_d(X)=\inf_f\ \sup_{y\in K}\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}_X f^{-1}(y),$$ where the infimum runs over continuous maps $f:X\to K$ to simplicial complexes of dimension at most $d$. The complexes have their usual polyhedral topology. The diameter is that of the entire fiber, including all its components; empty fibers may be omitted.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $n\ge4$, there is a finite constant $C_n$ such that every connected complete smooth Riemannian $n$-manifold $(M,g)$, without boundary and with $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge1$, admits a continuous map $f:M\to K$ to a simplicial complex of dimension at most $n-2$ satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}_g f^{-1}(y)\le C_n\qquad(y\in K).$$ In particular, $\mathop{\mathrm{UW}}_{n-2}(M,g)\le C_n$.*

</div>

The constant is independent of $M$, its topology, and its geometry. The theorem applies to compact and noncompact manifolds, with no orientability, spin, covering, or bounded geometry assumption. If $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge\sigma^2>0$, applying the theorem to $\sigma^2g$ gives $\mathop{\mathrm{UW}}_{n-2}(M,g)\le C_n/\sigma$.

For a closed connected Riemannian manifold, let $\operatorname{FillRad}(M,g)$ be Gromov’s filling radius (M. Gromov 1983) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[gromov1983Filling](#ref-gromov1983Filling)], defined by the Kuratowski embedding and the fundamental class, with coefficients in $\mathbb Z$ if $M$ is orientable and in $\mathbb Z_2$ otherwise (Nabutovsky, Rotman, and Sabourau 2021, Definition 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[nabutovskyRotmanSabourau2021](#ref-nabutovskyRotmanSabourau2021)]. The next corollary gives the closed-manifold case in dimensions at least four of Gromov’s filling-radius Conjecture $A^{-}$ (Misha Gromov 2017, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[gromov2017Dozen](#ref-gromov2017Dozen)].

<a id="stmt-cor-filling-radius"></a>
<div id="cor:filling-radius" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Closed-manifold filling-radius bound). *Let $(M,g)$ be a closed connected smooth Riemannian $n$-manifold with $n\ge4$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Scal}}_g\ge\sigma^2$ for some $\sigma>0$. Then $$\operatorname{FillRad}(M,g)\le \frac{C_n}{2\sigma},$$ where $C_n$ is the constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-filling-radius"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-filling-radius</summary>

*Proof.* Gromov’s comparison, in the form recorded by Nabutovsky, Rotman, and Sabourau (Nabutovsky, Rotman, and Sabourau 2021, Definition 6.1 and equation (6.1)) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[nabutovskyRotmanSabourau2021](#ref-nabutovskyRotmanSabourau2021)], uses the same continuous-map and entire-fiber width convention as above. A target of dimension at most $n-2$ is also admissible for $\mathop{\mathrm{UW}}_{n-1}$. Since $M$ is closed, the comparison and the scaled form of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> give $$\operatorname{FillRad}(M,g)
 \le \tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{UW}}_{n-1}(M,g)
 \le \tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{UW}}_{n-2}(M,g)
 \le \frac{C_n}{2\sigma}.$$ ◻

</details>

For the closed-cover consequence we also use the *continuous macroscopic dimension*: $\dim_{\mathrm{mc}}X\le d$ means that there is a continuous map to a simplicial complex of dimension at most $d$, with its uniform simplex metric and a finite uniform bound on the diameters of its entire fibers. Each simplex has the same standard Euclidean shape and edge length.

<a id="stmt-cover-main"></a>
<div id="cover:main" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Continuous macroscopic dimension of universal covers). *Let $(M,h)$ be a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension $n\ge2$ with positive scalar curvature. Its universal cover, with the lifted original metric $\widetilde h$, satisfies $$\dim_{\mathrm{mc}}(\widetilde M,\widetilde h)\le n-2.$$*

</div>

<a id="stmt-cor-aspherical"></a>
<div id="cor:aspherical" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (Aspherical obstruction). *No closed smooth aspherical manifold of dimension $n\ge4$ admits a Riemannian metric of strictly positive scalar curvature.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-aspherical"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-aspherical</summary>

*Proof.* It suffices to consider a connected manifold; passing to its orientation double cover if necessary preserves the hypotheses. Suppose such a metric exists. Its scalar curvature has a positive minimum, so the lifted metric on the contractible universal cover $X$ is complete with a uniform positive scalar-curvature lower bound. The deck group acts isometrically and cocompactly. The scaled form of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives a continuous map from $X$ to an $(n-2)$-complex with uniformly bounded entire fibers. The width and filling-radius implications in Gromov’s noncompact argument $A^+\Rightarrow B$ (Misha Gromov 2017, sec. 3, pp. 6–8) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[gromov2017Dozen](#ref-gromov2017Dozen)] exclude such a map on a contractible universal cover with a cocompact deck action. This gives the contradiction. ◻

</details>

An independent proof in every dimension follows from the stronger rational-inessentiality theorem of OpenAI (2026, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[openai2026RationalInessentiality](#ref-openai2026RationalInessentiality)].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and significance

Gromov introduced macroscopic dimension in his study of positive scalar curvature (M. Gromov 1996, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[gromov1996](#ref-gromov1996)]. His quantitative conjecture already asks whether a complete manifold with a positive scalar-curvature lower bound can be mapped continuously to a polyhedron of codimension two with all point inverses bounded at the curvature scale (M. Gromov 1996§$2\frac12$) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[gromov1996](#ref-gromov1996)]; see also (Misha Gromov 2019, 3180, Comments after Conjecture 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[gromov2019Mean](#ref-gromov2019Mean)]. The diameter condition concerns each entire fiber, including all its components. In the universal cover of a closed manifold the corresponding macroscopic-dimension assertion asks only for some finite bound. The continuous invariant and the version requiring a Lipschitz map to a uniformly metrized complex are distinct (Dranishnikov 2013, Definitions 1.1–1.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[dranishnikov2014](#ref-dranishnikov2014)].

The pointwise curvature hypothesis cannot be replaced by its fixed-scale macroscopic counterpart. Kumar and Sen construct closed manifolds in every dimension at least four with a prescribed positive fixed-scale macroscopic scalar-curvature lower bound and arbitrarily large Urysohn codimension-two width (Kumar and Sen 2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[kumarSen2026Width](#ref-kumarSen2026Width)].

The low-dimensional cases have a substantial earlier history. Gromov and Lawson obtained geometric width bounds for closed three-manifolds (Mikhael Gromov and Lawson 1983, Corollary 10.11) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[gromovLawson1983](#ref-gromovLawson1983)], and Bolotov proved that the universal cover of a closed three-manifold cannot have macroscopic dimension two (D. V. Bolotov 2003) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[bolotov2003](#ref-bolotov2003)]. For closed three-manifolds with positive scalar curvature, Liokumovich and Maximo constructed Morse functions with curvature-scale bounds for the areas and ambient diameters of connected level components (Liokumovich and Maximo 2023, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[liokumovichMaximo2023](#ref-liokumovichMaximo2023)] <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[liokumovichWang2023](#ref-liokumovichWang2023)]. Liokumovich and Wang constructed proper Morse functions on complete noncompact three-manifolds with uniformly positive scalar curvature, with uniform diameter bounds for connected level components (Liokumovich and Wang 2026, Theorem 1.1). We use that result and an explicit passage to the Reeb graph for the three-dimensional closed-cover consequence; the surface case follows from the Bonnet–Myers argument.

The aspherical obstruction in dimensions four and five was established by Chodosh and Li (Chodosh and Li 2024, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[chodoshLi](#ref-chodoshLi)], with an independent proof in dimension five by Gromov (Misha Gromov 2020, sec. 7, Main Corollary) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[gromov2020Aspherical](#ref-gromov2020Aspherical)].

For the macroscopic-dimension conjecture, Bolotov proved the closed spin case under the Strong Novikov Conjecture and a cohomological-dimension condition (D. Bolotov 2009) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[bolotov2009](#ref-bolotov2009)]. Bolotov and Dranishnikov then obtained the codimension-two conclusion for closed spin manifolds under the Strong Novikov Conjecture and injectivity of the connective-to-periodic real K-homology map (D. Bolotov and Dranishnikov 2010, Theorem 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[bolotovDranishnikov2010](#ref-bolotovDranishnikov2010)]. For closed orientable totally non-spin manifolds of dimension at least five, they also showed that a macroscopic dimension drop by one implies a drop by two (D. Bolotov and Dranishnikov 2016, Theorem 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[bolotovDranishnikov2016](#ref-bolotovDranishnikov2016)].
