Thus no sum of normal cap derivatives, and no extra cap potential, enters the budget. For a minus update negate $u_E$ and take a minimum with the old profile. The strict anchor regions for opposite signs are disjoint and contain no opposite old poles by <a href="#local:anchors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="local:anchors">48</a>.

Write $T_i^+$ for the end of the $i$th cap crossing. Enlarge the plus core by the open set $$\{x:\delta_B(x)<\varepsilon/2\}\times
       \prod_i(T_i^++b_i,\infty),\qquad b_i>0.$$ The properly embedded closed tails ensure that its closure in the coface has $\delta_B\le\varepsilon/2$ and $t_i\ge T_i^++b_i$. It is therefore contained in the open set $\{\delta_B<\varepsilon\}\times\prod_i(T_i^+,\infty)$, where every branch of the minimum is $+\infty$. This proves closure confinement, including at the cap fronts, with the corresponding statement for a minus core. The front positions are also chosen beyond the exact anchor-product thresholds, so its support retains the designated strict anchor sign.

Use the same caps and cutoffs for the same normal coordinates in every identified occurrence. On a face obtained by hitting one of these coordinates, that coordinate’s cap is eventually $+\infty$ and its cutoff is one, and that factor disappears from <a href="#shield:cap-formula" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shield:cap-formula">[shield:cap-formula]</a>. If an unrelated coordinate is hit, an intersection sufficiently far in its collar would require $x$ to escape into a further face of $B$, which the compact support forbids. Finite incidence allows all relevant intersection thresholds to be exceeded at once. These observations prove compatibility for all chains and show why a commuting product occurrence is not counted more than once. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-shield-global"></a>
<div id="shield:global" class="proposition">

**Proposition 59** (The global shielding output). *At a fixed stage $r$, let $j_0$ be the current label and keep its true minimizers fixed. There are updates of every remaining profile and core, and compatible smooth additions $D_j\ge0$, with the following properties on every parent packet $E$ of dimension $m$:*

<a id="shield:profile-output"></a>
1.  *The updated profiles have locally Lipschitz finite truncations and satisfy, almost everywhere on their finite bands, $$\label{shield:profile-output}
     2\abs{\nabla\mu'_j}\le a_r(\mu'_j)^2+\Pi_j+D_j.$$ All these data are exact compatible products. Each core only enlarges, and its closure remains in the interior of its designated updated pole set. The original forced sets and the anchor sign restrictions are preserved.*

2.  *With $G'_j=E\setminus(U_j^{\prime+}\cup U_j^{\prime-})$, the set <a id="shield:guard-output"></a>$$\label{shield:guard-output}
     H_E^*=\left\{x:\#\{j\ne j_0:x\in G'_j\}\ge m-2\right\}$$ is closed and misses $\Sigma_E$. In an exact collar over a face $B$, its projection is contained in $H_B^*$. This holds for every further face and every simultaneous product limit.*

<a id="shield:form-output"></a>
3.  *For one common $\theta_*<.9$, all walls and joined sides satisfy $$\label{shield:form-output}
     \int 1.16\sum_{j\ne j_0}D_jv^2
     \le\theta_*\bigl(\mathcal N(v)+e_0\norm v_2^2\bigr)
     \le .9\bigl(\mathcal N(v)+e_0\norm v_2^2\bigr).$$ The additions are bounded with their derivatives on each post-radial packet. There are only finitely many local additions on each such packet and only finitely many extensions into it.*

*For the initial radial parent the same conclusions hold with locally finite additions over its compact transition shells; boundedness is asserted on the corresponding compact blocks and <a href="#shield:form-output" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shield:form-output">[shield:form-output]</a> holds on every compact test. No convergence assertion at an end of the original manifold is needed.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-shield-global"><summary>Proof of stmt-shield-global</summary>

*Proof.* First consider the packet system after the radial cut. Process its finite set of depths from the deepest faces upward. The anchors and thresholds of <a href="#local:anchors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="local:anchors">48</a> are fixed once, before any updates. Initially the cumulative addition is zero. Maintain a single common upper form bound at least $.78$; it is harmless to start this bookkeeping bound at $.78$ although the initial form vanishes. Enumerate the countable pairs consisting of a packet and a possible successive local addition number by distinct integers $\nu\ge1$. Reserve $\gamma_\nu=.1\,2^{-\nu}$ for that pair, allocating at most half to <a href="#shield:maximum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shield:maximum">56</a> and half to the simultaneous extensions of <a href="#shield:extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shield:extension">57</a>. The common bound after any collection of completed updates is at most $$.78+\sum_{\nu\text{ used}}\gamma_\nu\le .88<.9.$$ We may consequently take $\theta_*=.88$. There is no requirement that a shell scale or its extension fronts be uniform in the packet or in the addition number.

Suppose all strict further faces of $E$ have been treated and their updates extended. The remaining risky set $$K_E=\Sigma_E\cap H_E^*$$ is compact. To prove this, if points in it escaped, the packet escape property <a href="#packet:escape" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="packet:escape">33</a> would give a subsequence over a deeper face $B$, with compact convergent base coordinates and every escaping normal coordinate tending to infinity. The updated cores are exact products there. There are finitely many relevant labels, so pass to a further subsequence with a fixed subset of $m-2$ undecided labels. Membership in each of their closed sets $G'_j$ passes to the base limit. Thus the limit base point has at least $m-2$ undecided labels. This implies membership in $H_B^*$, because $\dim B-2\le m-2$. By <a href="#cut:end-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cut:end-convergence">43</a> the translated true cuts converge to the true cut over $B$ times the normal Euclidean factor. Support convergence puts the limiting base point on the true boundary over $B$. A regular limiting boundary point would have a neighborhood of smooth graphical convergence, which contains no singular points of the approximating walls. The limit must therefore be singular on the face. This contradicts its completed shielding. Thus $K_E$ has no escaping sequence; it is closed, and the compact-part property proves compactness. If $m<8$ it is already empty.

Denote this initial compact set by $K_E^{(0)}$. For each remaining label and each strict anchor sign, cover $K_E^{(0)}$ in that open region by the countable compact flat pieces of <a href="#local:flat-pieces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="local:flat-pieces">49</a>. These pieces need not themselves be an open cover. Enumerate the signed pieces; their eventual inner forcing neighborhoods will provide the open cover needed for termination. For each, choose its shell sufficiently small by <a href="#shield:maximum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shield:maximum">56</a>, then hold that scale fixed and extend its caps, potential, and core by [shield:extension,shield:caps] [shield:extension](#shield:extension), [shield:caps](#shield:caps). Supports can be as small as needed to stay within the prescribed strict anchor region. Opposite updates for one label therefore never conflict. Profiles retain <a href="#shield:profile-output" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shield:profile-output">[shield:profile-output]</a> by their active-branch inequalities, and cores enlarge monotonically.

To prove finite termination, after $N$ additions set $$O_N=\{x\in E:\#\{j\ne j_0:x\in G_j^{(N)}\}<m-2\},
 \qquad K_E^{(N)}=K_E^{(0)}\setminus O_N.$$ Each $O_N$ is open: with finitely many labels its complement is a finite union of intersections of closed $G_j^{(N)}$. Core enlargement implies $O_N\subset O_{N+1}$. Fix $x\in K_E^{(0)}$. For each of its strict anchors, some enumerated compact piece contains $x$, and the addition on that piece puts $x$ in an open enlarged core, irrespective of how small its positive shell scale was chosen. Only finitely many anchors are involved. Once those additions have occurred, only threshold-equality labels can remain undecided at $x$. By <a href="#local:anchors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="local:anchors">48</a> there are at most $m-8<m-2$ of those. Thus $x\in O_N$ for some finite $N$. The increasing open sets $O_N$ cover $K_E^{(0)}$, so compactness supplies a finite subcover; taking its largest index gives $K_E^{(N)}=\varnothing$. Stop there. This argument proves an actual finite stopping time before forming any infinite sum of profiles. All subsequent updates only shrink the guard.

There may be countably many packets at one depth. An originating packet has finitely many directed coface occurrences, so each individual extension has already been chosen in finitely many places. Conversely, a fixed target packet has finitely many source faces, including through chains, so only finitely many packets at that depth affect it. Each of those sources required finitely many additions. This uses both directions of finite incidence from <a href="#packet:category" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="packet:category">16</a>. Its updated functions are finite sums or finite minima and maxima; there is no infinite limiting profile on that packet. Its product thresholds are bounded by finitely many chosen thresholds, and all its potential derivatives remain bounded. The common form bound after a whole depth follows from the same summable allowance. This justifies proceeding to the next depth, and proves the asserted smoothness, compatibility, and form bounds throughout the system.

The new guard is closed because the number of labels is finite and each $G'_j$ is closed. On a far collar, exact product cores give the same count upstairs and downstairs. Lower face dimension weakens the required count, proving the projection assertion in (ii). These are precisely the closed-set and product-limit properties needed for the guarded regularization in <a href="#sec:regularization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:regularization">13</a>; a quantitative smooth tube is obtained there from regular graphical convergence.

For the initial radial cut, process its locally finite compact transition shells in order, choosing the supports of all additions inside buffered neighborhoods of their respective shells. By [packet:initialization,cut:barriers] [packet:initialization](#packet:initialization), [cut:barriers](#cut:barriers), the barrier subbands separate these neighborhoods, and the true boundary lies in their interiors. The true singular set in a shell is closed inside its compact buffered annulus, and the guard is closed. Their intersection is therefore compact. Apply the same increasing-open-cover argument there, keeping the closure of every forcing region inside this one shell buffer. There is no coface extension at this step. Before any addition only finitely many previous additions have been made, so their total is a bounded compactly supported potential and <a href="#shield:maximum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shield:maximum">56</a> applies. The base localization is obtained from a proper distance cutoff; all estimates after localization use only fixed compact data. Use summable allowances as before. Local finiteness leaves smooth potentials and well-defined profiles, and a compact test sees only finitely many shell additions. The form bound therefore persists after all shells are treated. This proves the radial assertion and completes the construction. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:regularization"></a>
### Regularization and restoration of the curvature invariant

The minimizing partition is smooth away from a singular set, whereas the next cutting step needs smooth complete packets. Shielding has made the closed guard disjoint from the singular set; the first lemma below obtains the regular tubes needed for comparison. We construct globally smooth partitions that accurately approximate the true partitions near these guards. We then transfer the scalar form to the new walls and joined sides, smooth the cylinder seams, and convert the form bound into a pointwise bound by adding circle factors. Throughout this section a stage $r$ and its current label $j_0$ are fixed. The profiles and cores of the remaining labels have already been updated by [shield:global](#shield:global). Write $$\begin{gathered}
 \widetilde\Pi_j=\Pi_j+D_j,\qquad
 G'_j=E\setminus(U_j^{+,\mathrm{updated}}\cup U_j^{-,\mathrm{updated}}),\\
 H_E^*=\left\{x\in E:
       \#\{j\in J_r\setminus\{j_0\}:x\in G'_j\}\ge m-2\right\}.
\end{gathered}$$ Here $m=\dim E$ is the parent base dimension. The set $H_E^*$ is closed and misses the singular set of the true boundary. A nonpositive counting threshold means the whole packet. Scalar curvatures, mean curvatures, Laplacians, and measures include the old torus factors; functions and tests are invariant in those factors. Constants in this section may depend on a packet and its finite incidence diagram. At the first, radial step the corresponding constants are assigned on enlarged compact radial blocks. In particular no uniform geometry bound for the original manifold is used.

Use adjacent parameters $0\le t_r<t_{r+1}\le1$, and put $$\Delta_r=t_{r+1}-t_r,\qquad
 a_r=.60+.03t_r,\qquad b_r=1.12-.05t_r,\qquad
 \lambda_r=.25-t_r/8,$$ $$\eta_r=\Delta_r/200,\qquad
 e_0=(\lambda_r-\lambda_{r+1})/100=\Delta_r/800,
 \qquad C=3.99.$$ Only positivity of the particular increment is needed; none of the geometric choices is required to be uniform in $r$. The proofs below are stated for finite label data. Their constant-preserving form, spelled out in [regular:constant-preservation](#regular:constant-preservation), will also be used for a finite exceptional list together with a summable constant tail.

<a id="pm-h-40"></a>
#### Guarded partitions and transfer of the raw form

<a id="stmt-regular-guards"></a>
<div id="regular:guards" class="lemma">

**Lemma 60** (Uniform control on the guarded region). *Fix a finite integer $J\ge4$. On each parent packet $E$, let $\varsigma_E$ be signed distance to the true boundary support, truncated at $\pm1$, positive in the plus phase. If the support is empty on a component, use the corresponding constant sign. After the radial split, the following choices can be made on each whole parent packet.*

1.  *There is a positive $\rho_E<1/10$ such that near $$\{x:\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,H_E^*)<4\rho_E,\ |\varsigma_E(x)|<4\rho_E\}$$ the true boundary is regular, has an embedded normal tube, and signed distance has bounded derivatives through order $J+2$ on a slightly smaller tube. The current forced core closures have positive uniform distance from the true boundary.*

2.  *On an elementary collar $B\times(T,\infty)$, signed distances converge uniformly to the pullback of $\varsigma_B$ as $T\to\infty$. They converge in $C^J$ on the guarded tubes, with the coface tube chosen inside the face’s tube. These statements are uniform over the whole face, including its noncompact ends.*

*For the initial radial parent, assertion <span class="upright">(i)</span> holds separately on each enlarged compact radial block, with its own radius and derivative bounds; assertion <span class="upright">(ii)</span> is not required. The radii depend on the true partitions and the updated guards, and can be chosen before any smooth-approximation tolerances.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-guards"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-guards</summary>

*Proof.* On a compact set, closedness of $H_E^*$ and its disjointness from the singular set give a regular neighborhood of every point where the guard meets the boundary. A finite subcover and the normal inverse function theorem give a positive tube radius and all the asserted finite-order bounds there. The same compactness argument applies to the separation of the current core closures, which lie inside the barrier domains of [cut:pressure](#cut:pressure), [cut:gmt](#cut:gmt) and whose neighborhoods miss the support.

We explain why these statements remain uniform at infinity. If a sequence escapes in $E$, the finite face description in [packet:escape](#packet:escape) gives, after passage to a subsequence, a product window over a further face $F$ in which the $F$ coordinates converge in a compact set and all the remaining normal coordinates tend to infinity. Bounded metric balls lie in these product windows eventually. The true phases converge there to the product of the true phase of $F$, by [cut:end-convergence](#cut:end-convergence). The updated cores and their complements are exact products. Consequently a limit of guarded points lies in $H_F^*$: a count of at least $m-2$ is at least $\dim F-2$. The limiting boundary point is therefore regular. Smooth, single-sheet convergence near this point supplies an embedded normal tube of a fixed positive radius, and excludes additional support in a smaller neighborhood. In particular no sequence at distance tending to zero from both the guard and the support can have degenerating tube radius or unbounded prescribed finite derivatives. This proves the first assertion by contradiction, with buffers reduced if necessary. The barrier statement at infinity follows in the same way from its face statement and phase/support convergence.

For uniform convergence in an entire elementary collar, suppose a discrepancy persists at $(x_i,t_i)$ with $t_i\to\infty$. If $x_i$ stays in a compact part of $B$, local product convergence gives a contradiction. Otherwise descend through the finite face diagram of $B$. Commutation of its collars with the $t$ collar puts both the partition of $E$ and that of $B$ over the same further face $F$, with compact tangential coordinates and all escaping coordinates tending to infinity. Both limiting partitions are the same unique product partition. Local support convergence implies convergence of truncated distances: a nearest point at distance less than $1$ is detected in a slightly larger fixed ball, and if there is no such point both truncated distances approach the saturated value. Away from the limiting support, phase convergence fixes the sign. This proves uniform $C^0$ convergence. On a guarded tube the limiting boundary is regular; smooth graph convergence and the normal inverse function theorem give $C^J$ convergence of signed distance on smaller tubes.

Choose radii from the deepest faces upward. Each coface radius is smaller than a fixed fraction of every relevant further-face radius. For example, a point within $\rho_E$ of $H_E^*$ projects, far out, to within $2\rho_E$ of $H_B^*$, and $|\varsigma_E|<\rho_E$ implies $|\varsigma_B|<2\rho_E$ there. These are statements about the fixed true geometry and exact cores, without approximation tolerances. This order also handles a simultaneous escape in several coordinates and proves the last assertion. At the radial step the same proof on compact blocks requires no assertion about an initial spatial end. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-regular-partition"></a>
<div id="regular:partition" class="lemma">

**Lemma 61** (Compatible smooth partitions). *There are compatible smooth defining functions $g_E$ with $0$ a regular value, positive and negative on the respective current forced core closures, and with $L_E=g_E^{-1}(0)\subset\operatorname{int}G_{j_0}$. After the radial split they can be chosen uniformly as close as prescribed to $\varsigma_E$ in value and in $C^J$ on smaller guarded tubes. In a buffered neighborhood of $H_E^*$, a smooth diffeomorphism arbitrarily close to the identity in $C^{J-1}$ identifies the smooth partition with the true partition. It uses the same map on the wall and on the boundaries of its two sides. At the initial radial step, the approximation tolerances and comparison buffers are chosen separately on enlarged compact radial blocks. The defining functions, but not this comparison map, are exact products in sufficiently far old collars.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-partition"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-partition</summary>

*Proof.* First choose all the geometric radii in [regular:guards](#regular:guards). Prescribe positive value and $C^J$ error targets on the guarded regions, small enough also to preserve the core signs. Allocate smaller targets to faces to meet the requests of all their cofaces, including through chains, as in [packet:approximation](#packet:approximation). There are only finitely many such requests per face and finite depth. This allocation does not change any tube radius.

Work now from deeper faces upward. Smooth $\varsigma_E$ by local convolution and a smooth partition of unity, with arbitrarily small value error everywhere and derivative error on the regular buffers. These errors can be prescribed separately: use the smooth function itself on an inner regular buffer and blend to a value approximation outside a larger one. On a far collar blend to the previously chosen face function. [regular:guards](#regular:guards) makes the discrepancy from the face’s true signed distance arbitrarily small by moving the collar start. The discrepancy of the already chosen face function from that distance is instead paid from its preallocated error target. These two errors are kept separate.

More explicitly, take cutoffs $f_i$ from $0$ to $1$ in the elementary normal coordinates, in valid far product ranges. Use weights $$w_0=\prod_i(1-f_i),\qquad
 w_i=f_i\prod_{a<i}(1-f_a).$$ They sum to $1$. All derivatives through order $J$ are bounded by constants fixed before the targets are allocated (translated cutoffs have the same bounds). Thus blending costs a fixed multiple of the participating errors. Face copies agree on all relevant overlaps once the starts exceed their product thresholds. When any $f_i=1$, every participating copy equals that face copy on the overlap; hence the result is exactly compatible. This proves the claimed approximations.

Near a possible zero in a guarded tube, the defining function has nonzero normal derivative and its zero is a small normal graph over the true wall. Off the tube, uniform value closeness preserves the phase where its sign is required. The copied end functions already have regular zeros. On the remaining compact region, a relative regular-value perturbation makes all zeros regular without changing the copied ends. For completeness, if $g$ is the preliminary function, choose a smooth $\chi\ge0$, positive on its nonregular zeros and zero near the regions to be kept fixed. For an arbitrarily small regular value $c$ of $-g/\chi$ on $\{\chi>0\}$, the function $g+c\chi$ has regular zeros there. Where $\chi=0$, its differential is that of $g$, since $d\chi=0$ there. Bounded derivatives of $\chi$ and small $c$ preserve all strict error and sign requirements. On the initial space use a positive smooth $\chi$ with locally prescribed small derivatives and the same regular-value argument; the radial separators are preserved.

If the perturbed wall is $t=\alpha(x)$ in a true normal tube, the map $$(x,t)\longmapsto (x,t-\chi_1(x,t)\alpha(x))$$ with $\chi_1=1$ on the needed graph region and supported inside a larger regular buffer is a diffeomorphism for sufficiently small graph norm. It carries the graph to $t=0$, preserves its two local sides, and tends to the identity in $C^{J-1}$. Choose the buffer cutoff on the regular wall slightly beyond the projection of the smaller guard neighborhood and extend by the identity. Uniform regular charts give bounded cutoff derivatives. The same formula restricts to the wall used as a cylinder base. No product property of this auxiliary comparison is asserted or needed. ◻

</details>

Perform the split of [packet:split](#packet:split) using these defining functions. For the moment use the raw metric: the old metric on the bulk and the frozen induced metric on each fresh infinite cylinder. Let $p_A$ denote restriction/inclusion on a wall or bulk and cylinder projection on a fresh cylinder. Pull back the remaining profiles, cores, and $\widetilde\Pi_j$ by $p_A$. These data are continuous at a seam and compatible in old ends. Define the new required set $D_A$ from the remaining cores and the new base dimension. The raw forms of [cut:raw-domination](#cut:raw-domination) were defined on the true minimizing wall and its joined sides. We first estimate those forms, then use the comparison map to transfer the estimate to the raw metrics of the smooth split just constructed.

<a id="stmt-regular-transfer"></a>
<div id="regular:transfer" class="lemma">

**Lemma 62** (Counts and transfer of the raw form). *The defining-function accuracies can be chosen so that on a uniform neighborhood of $D_A$ in each new packet the raw scalar form controls <a id="regular:raw-target"></a>$$\label{regular:raw-target}
 \lambda_{r+1}+(1+3\eta_r)b_{r+1}\sum_{j\ne j_0}\widetilde\Pi_j,$$ with a strictly positive mass and gradient reserve. The form has gradient coefficient $C=3.99$ and includes the seam term $2\int H_{\rm out}u(0)^2$ on a joined side. Tests are compactly supported, with matching seam traces.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-transfer"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-transfer</summary>

*Proof.* For a side packet $\dim A=m$, a point in $D_A$ projects to at least $m-1$ remaining undecided labels, hence into $H_E^*$ and the parent’s old required set. For a hit packet $\dim A=m-1$, it projects to at least $m-2$ remaining undecided labels. The wall lies in $G_{j_0}$, which supplies the additional old undecided label. The projection is again in both required sets. These inclusions are unchanged on the fresh cylinder, where all relevant data are pulled back from its base. All these maps decrease lengths in the raw metrics; therefore a small neighborhood of $D_A$ projects into small neighborhoods of the two parent sets. The closed-set count, rather than a continuity claim for the counting function, is what is used here.

<a id="regular:eta"></a>
On compared true pieces in a sufficiently small neighborhood of the old required set, continuity and the bounded derivatives of the fixed parent data give $$R\ge\lambda_r+b_r\sum_j\Pi_j-e_0,
 \qquad e_0=\frac{\lambda_r-\lambda_{r+1}}{100}.$$ By [cut:raw-domination](#cut:raw-domination), [cut:reserve](#cut:reserve), the raw form on these pieces dominates $R-1.02\Pi_{j_0}$, with spare gradient coefficients $.94$ on bulk and $1.99$ on a wall or cylinder. The bound in [shield:global](#shield:global) pays for the additional potential $1.16\sum_{j\ne j_0}D_j$ from these spare gradient terms and a mass loss of at most $.9e_0$. Since $b_r>1.02$, dropping the remaining nonnegative current-label term leaves <a id="regular:true-budget"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 Q_{\rm true}(u)&\ge
 \int\left(\lambda_r-2e_0+
       b_r\sum_{j\ne j_0}\Pi_j+
       1.16\sum_{j\ne j_0}D_j\right)u^2
       +.2\int|\nabla u|^2.\label{regular:true-budget}
\end{aligned}$$ Indeed the unrounded gradient remainders are $.94-.9\cdot.8=.22$ and $1.99-.9\cdot1.7=.46$, and the mass loss is at most $1.9e_0$. The exact numerical margins are $$\label{regular:eta}
\begin{aligned}
 a_{r+1}-(1+\eta_r)a_r
   &=\frac{3\Delta_r(180-t_r)}{20000}
     \ge .02685\Delta_r>0,\\
 b_r-(1+3\eta_r)b_{r+1}
   &=\frac{\Delta_r[664+15(t_r+\Delta_r)]}{20000}
     \ge .0332\Delta_r>0,\\
 1.16-(1+3\eta_r)b_{r+1}
   &=\frac{800+1000t_r+664\Delta_r+
                  15\Delta_r(t_r+\Delta_r)}{20000}>0.
\end{aligned}$$ These identities use $0\le t_r<t_r+\Delta_r\le1$. Thus [regular:true-budget](#regular:true-budget) exceeds [regular:raw-target](#regular:raw-target) by at least $98e_0$ in mass and $.2$ in gradient, in addition to strictly positive potential margins.

Use the comparison diffeomorphism of [regular:partition](#regular:partition) on bulk and on the common wall/cylinder base, leaving the cylinder coordinate and torus variables unchanged. A test supported in a smaller buffered neighborhood of $D_A$ becomes a compact matching test on the true regular pieces, extended by zero outside the comparison region. For a test in one component set it to zero on other components. In the bulk away from the wall the map is the identity and the phase signs already agree. All supports stay away from singular points and their cylinders.

The pulled-back metric, volume weight, potentials, intrinsic stabilized wall curvature, and outward mean curvature converge uniformly with the chosen finite derivatives. For example the induced scalar curvature can be computed from Gauss’ equation, so the second fundamental form and the ambient curvature give the requisite bounds. Consequently, for arbitrarily small $\delta>0$ the discrepancy of the compared forms is bounded by $$\delta\left[\int(u^2+|\nabla u|^2)+\int_{\rm seam}u(0)^2\right].$$ The last term is controlled using the first unit interval of the raw product cylinder: <a id="regular:trace"></a>$$\label{regular:trace}
 \int_{\rm seam}u(0)^2
 \le 2\int_{S\times[0,1]}(u^2+|\partial_tu|^2).$$ This follows by writing $u(0)=u(t)-\int_0^t\partial_tu$ and integrating in $t$. It needs no zero boundary condition. Choosing all approximation errors sufficiently small leaves, for example, a reserve of $90e_0$ in mass and $.15$ in gradient. These choices can be prescribed on parent buffers before the individual new components are known: the projection inclusions place every relevant point there. At the radial step the enlarged compact blocks supply the bounds; preserved separators trap each new component in one such block before cylinder attachment. Finally impose all coface requests through the finite tolerance allocation already used in [regular:partition](#regular:partition). ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-41"></a>
#### Seam smoothing and restriction of profiles

The proof below uses scalar-curvature concentration at a mean-curvature jump, as in the corner smoothing of Miao (2002, secs. 2–3) <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[miao2002Corners](#ref-miao2002Corners)]. Here the smoothing width varies tangentially, and the form estimate must preserve compatible product ends.

<a id="stmt-regular-seam"></a>
<div id="regular:seam" class="lemma">

**Lemma 63** (Smoothing a seam with variable width). *The raw joined metrics admit smooth compatible metrics with arbitrarily small $C^0$ error, and their scalar forms converge to the raw forms in the following quantitative sense. On each joined packet and for every $\delta>0$, the smoothing can be chosen so that the absolute form error is at most $$\delta\int(u^2+|\nabla u|^2)$$ for every compact matching test, using the raw metric to measure the right side. The old warped-product structure and every sufficiently far old product end are preserved. The choices on a wall can satisfy the requests of both incident sides at once.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-seam"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-seam</summary>

*Proof.* In a normal collar of a smooth wall write the old stabilized metric as $ds^2+\beta_s$ for $s\le0$, where $s$ increases outward. Here $\beta_s$ includes the old torus factors. The raw joined metric is $ds^2+\beta_{\min(s,0)}$. Choose a smooth concave function $\Phi$ equal to $t$ for $t\le-1$ and to $0$ for $t\ge1$, with $0\le\Phi'\le1$. Such a function is obtained by integrating a smooth decreasing step function symmetric about $0$. It satisfies $\Phi\le0$ and $\int\Phi''=-1= -\int|\Phi''|$.

For a smooth positive function $\epsilon(x)$ on the wall put $$\phi(x,s)=\epsilon(x)\Phi(s/\epsilon(x)),\qquad
 G_\epsilon=ds^2+\beta_{\phi(x,s)}.$$ It equals the raw metric outside $|s|<\epsilon(x)$ and differs from it in $C^0$ by $O(\sup\epsilon)$. In a fixed tangential chart the extra derivatives of $\phi$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned}
 |\partial_x\phi|&\le c|d\epsilon|,\\
 |\partial_x^2\phi|&\le
 c\bigl(|d^2\epsilon|+|d\epsilon|^2/\epsilon\bigr).
\end{aligned}$$ Choose $\epsilon=\zeta^2$ with $\zeta>0$ as small in $C^2$ as prescribed. Then $|d\epsilon|^2/\epsilon=4|d\zeta|^2$ and $d^2\epsilon=2\zeta d^2\zeta+2d\zeta\otimes d\zeta$. Thus these tangential errors tend to zero. On a packet, positive compatible choices have a positive infimum and bounded derivatives by the compact-part/product-end description. Their size, but not the resulting higher metric bounds, can be made arbitrarily small. Normal collar widths are also positive packetwise by the same description.

Let $B^{\rm old}_\tau,H^{\rm old}_\tau$ be the slice second fundamental form and mean curvature of $ds^2+\beta_s$. Exactly, $$B_s=\phi_sB^{\rm old}_{\phi},\qquad
 H_s=\phi_sH^{\rm old}_{\phi}.$$ For a metric $ds^2+\beta(x,s)$ the Gauss and normal variation identities give $$R(G_\epsilon)=R(\beta(x,s))-|B_s|^2-H_s^2-2\partial_sH_s.$$ The tangential derivative bounds just proved show that all terms on the strip are uniformly bounded except <a id="regular:spike"></a>$$\label{regular:spike}
 -2\phi_{ss}H^{\rm old}_{\phi}.$$ In particular the formula differentiates $H_s$ only in $s$; potentially large mixed $xs$ derivatives do not give another scalar spike. The remaining contribution from this derivative is $-2\phi_s^2\partial_\tau H^{\rm old}_\phi$, which is bounded.

Let $J_\epsilon(x,s)$ be the density of $\beta_\phi$ relative to the zero slice. Uniformly on the strip, $J_\epsilon=1+O(\epsilon)$ and $H^{\rm old}_\phi=H^{\rm old}_0+O(\epsilon)$. For each fixed $x$, $$\int-\phi_{ss}\,ds=1,\qquad
 \int|\phi_{ss}|\,ds=1.$$ Writing $E_x=\int_{-\epsilon(x)}^{\epsilon(x)}|\partial_su|^2ds$, the fundamental theorem of calculus gives $$|u(s)-u(0)|^2\le\epsilon(x)E_x,
 \quad
 |u(s)^2-u(0)^2|
 \le2\sqrt{\epsilon(x)}|u(0)|E_x^{1/2}+\epsilon(x)E_x.$$ After integrating against $|\phi_{ss}|ds$, the error in replacing $u(s)^2$ by $u(0)^2$ is at most $c\sqrt{\sup\epsilon}\,(u(0)^2+E_x)$, for $\sup\epsilon\le1$. The density and mean-curvature errors cost at most $c\sup\epsilon\,(u(0)^2+E_x)$. Thus the form contribution of [regular:spike](#regular:spike) converges to $2\int H^{\rm old}_0u(0)^2$, with the outward sign required in [cut:side-estimate](#cut:side-estimate).

The bounded strip terms have vanishing form cost because $$\int_{-\epsilon(x)}^{\epsilon(x)}u(s)^2ds
 \le c\epsilon(x)u(0)^2+c\epsilon(x)^2E_x.$$ The metric and density errors in the gradient and fixed bounded potential terms tend to zero in $H^1$ form norm. Integrate these estimates in $x$ and use [regular:trace](#regular:trace); the stated error bound follows. One first proves it for smooth matching tests and then for the matching $H^1$ completion by density.

Choose $\zeta$ on new hit packets, from deeper faces upward, after propagating the two incident-side requests through their finite diagrams. The function is an exact pullback in old product ends, so the displayed smoothing is also an exact product there. It is unchanged far out in the fresh cylinder coordinate. Since the normal coordinate and old metric already split off every old normal factor, the operation commutes with all the inherited collar identifications. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-regular-constant-preservation"></a>
<div id="regular:constant-preservation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 64** (Approximation that reproduces constants). *A continuous scalar function on a packet can be approximated by smooth functions, with any prescribed positive value error, by operators that reproduce constants. For compatible input on a packet diagram, the approximations can be chosen compatible in all sufficiently far product ends of that diagram. If the input and all face copies used at a packet equal the same constant $c$, the result equals $c$ there exactly. The same assertion holds componentwise for a finite list of functions and for a list having only finitely many nonconstant entries on each prescribed finite directed face/coface diagram.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-constant-preservation"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-constant-preservation</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a locally finite coordinate cover, relatively compact smaller charts, and a smooth partition of unity $\{\psi_\alpha\}$ subordinate to the smaller charts. Extend each chart slightly and average the function against a smooth nonnegative kernel of integral one, at a sufficiently small chart-dependent scale. Call the resulting local function $T_\alpha f$. Uniform continuity on the chart closure makes $|T_\alpha f-f|$ smaller than the prescribed error on $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\psi_\alpha$. Thus $$Tf=\sum_\alpha\psi_\alpha T_\alpha f$$ is a smooth approximation with that error. Every local average sends $c$ to $c$, and $\sum\psi_\alpha=1$, so $Tc=c$ exactly. Locally prescribed derivative accuracy is obtained in the same way wherever $f$ is already smooth, using a slightly larger regular buffer.

Now use the finite tolerance allocation of [packet:approximation](#packet:approximation), and blend from deeper faces upward with the weights $w_i$ in the proof of [regular:partition](#regular:partition). Their sum is one. Consequently blending preserves a common constant as well as exact face identities. The finite exceptional union over the prescribed directed dependencies is chosen before applying this construction. For every other entry the entire local input and all participating face copies coincide with the same constant, so all its averaging and blending errors are zero. No minimum of tolerances over these constant entries is needed. On the initial radial space the construction is local on buffered compact blocks and uses a locally finite chart cover. It has the same constant-reproduction property there. ◻

</details>

On a new packet $A$, write $R_{\rm old}$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm old}$ for the scalar curvature and invariant Laplacian of the smoothed split metric with its retained torus factors, before any new circle factors are added.

<a id="stmt-regular-profiles"></a>
<div id="regular:profiles" class="lemma">

**Lemma 65** (Restricted profiles and smooth potentials). *After the seam smoothing the pulled-back profiles and cores satisfy the stage $r+1$ profile conditions with smooth compatible potentials $\Pi_j^{\rm new}$ chosen so that $$(1+\eta_r)\widetilde\Pi_j
 \le\Pi_j^{\rm new}\le(1+2\eta_r)\widetilde\Pi_j.$$ If <a id="regular:Vstar"></a>$$\label{regular:Vstar}
 V_A^*=\lambda_{r+1}+b_{r+1}\sum_{j\ne j_0}\Pi_j^{\rm new},\qquad
 L_A^0=-C\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm old}+R_{\rm old}-V_A^*,$$ then on some positive uniform neighborhood of $D_A$ its form is bounded below by a positive multiple of $\|u\|_2^2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-profiles"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-profiles</summary>

*Proof.* We justify restriction to a hypersurface, which does not follow merely by restricting an ambient almost-everywhere inequality. If $f$ is locally Lipschitz on an open Riemannian set and $|df|\le F$ almost everywhere for continuous $F$, then at every point its local Lipschitz slope is at most $F$. In a small coordinate ball, convolve $f$ on a slightly larger ball. The derivative bound of the convolutions is at most the supremum of $F$ there times a metric distortion factor tending to $1$ as the ball shrinks. Integrating this bound along straight segments, passing to the uniform limit, and shrinking the ball proves the assertion. It follows also that every rectifiable curve satisfies the corresponding integrated bound, by subdividing into such balls.

Apply this on each open finite band of a profile, with $F=(a_r\mu_j^2+\widetilde\Pi_j)/2$. Restriction to the wall, inclusion of bulk, and projection on a cylinder therefore preserve the bound everywhere in local slope, and hence almost everywhere for the restricted locally Lipschitz function. Finite truncations remain locally Lipschitz, including where a pole is present. Across a seam the same conclusion follows by splitting a path into its bulk and cylinder portions; traces agree. A sufficiently small $C^0$ metric change increases all these slopes by at most $1+\eta_r$.

For each label $j$, the raw potential has its own positive lower bound $p_j^*>0$. Apply [regular:constant-preservation](#regular:constant-preservation) to $(1+3\eta_r/2)\widetilde\Pi_j$, with strict value error smaller than $\eta_r p_j^*/2$. This proves the displayed relative bounds. Different labels may require different accuracies; a common positive lower bound for an infinite label list is unnecessary. When the raw potential is a spatial constant $c_j$ on the whole relevant incident diagram, choose its new value to be exactly $(1+3\eta_r/2)c_j$. By [regular:eta](#regular:eta), $$2|\nabla\mu_j|_{\rm new}
 \le (1+\eta_r)(a_r\mu_j^2+\widetilde\Pi_j)
 \le a_{r+1}\mu_j^2+\Pi_j^{\rm new}.$$ Continuous projections preserve the inclusion of every core closure inside its pole interior and preserve all prescribed $q$ constraints.

Choose the seam form error small relative to the reserves of [regular:transfer](#regular:transfer). The potential in [regular:Vstar](#regular:Vstar) is at most that in [regular:raw-target](#regular:raw-target); the extra $\eta_r$ supplies additional margin for the potential smoothing. Metric equivalence allows the required-set neighborhood to be slightly reduced after smoothing. Thus one may retain, for example, an $e_0\|u\|_2^2$ lower bound for the form of $L_A^0$ on tests in that neighborhood. No compatibility of the neighborhoods themselves is asserted. ◻

</details>

We have obtained a smooth packet metric and the next profile inequalities. The scalar lower bound is still only a quadratic-form bound near the new required set. We next extend its positive gap to the whole packet by a correction that vanishes near that set, solve a bounded elliptic equation, and approximate its solutions compatibly. The final group of $399$ circle factors converts this form information into the pointwise scalar inequality.

<a id="pm-h-42"></a>
#### Compatible corrections and positive supersolutions

At this point the smooth split packets have compact truncations and exact product ends by [packet:split](#packet:split). At the radial step the preserved separating bands give the compact initial pieces. Hence [packet:geometry](#packet:geometry) already applies to these metrics and retained warps, before the following correction is constructed. In particular all bounds used below hold separately on each whole packet; they are not assumptions on the original manifold.

<a id="stmt-regular-correction"></a>
<div id="regular:correction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 66** (A compatible correction with a global gap). *There are smooth nonnegative bounded compatible potentials $K_A$, with all derivatives bounded packetwise, such that $K_A=0$ in a positive uniform neighborhood of $D_A$ and <a id="regular:global-gap"></a>$$\label{regular:global-gap}
 Q_A(u):=\int\bigl(C|\nabla u|^2+
       (R_{\rm old}-V_A^*+K_A)u^2\bigr)
 \ge g_A\|u\|_2^2\qquad(g_A>0)$$ for every compact smooth test. Let $\omega_A^{\rm old}$ be the product of the retained circle length functions for the smoothed metric on $A$, before the new circle factors at this split are appended. With $K_A$ acting by multiplication, define $$L_A=L_A^0+K_A
     =-C\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm old}+R_{\rm old}-V_A^*+K_A.$$ Its quadratic form in the measure $\omega_A^{\rm old}\,\mathrm d\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_A$ is $Q_A$. In an exact end over $B$, the base metric is a product and every retained warp pulls back from $B$. Consequently $$\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{{\rm old},A}=\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{{\rm old},B}+\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm normal},
 \qquad L_A=L_B-C\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm normal}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-correction"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-correction</summary>

*Proof.* First disregard compatibility on a fixed packet. Put $W_A=R_{\rm old}-V_A^*$. Choose a smooth angle $\theta$ equal to $0$ near $D_A$ and $\pi/2$ outside the neighborhood of local positivity, with bounded derivatives and with the support of $\cos\theta$ inside that neighborhood. Uniform charts and the positive neighborhood width give such a function by smoothing a distance cutoff with buffers. Set $$K_{\rm ind}=J\sin^2\theta+C|\nabla\theta|^2,
 \qquad J>\max\{0,-\inf W_A\}.$$ The identity for the squared partition $\cos\theta,\sin\theta$ shows that $Q_A^0+K_{\rm ind}$ has gap $\gamma_A=\min\{e_0,J+\inf W_A\}>0$. Indeed the gradient partition error is exactly $C|\nabla\theta|^2u^2$; local positivity controls the cosine part and $W_A+J>0$ controls the sine part. The correction is zero on a smaller uniform neighborhood of $D_A$.

Proceed from deeper faces upward. In sufficiently far elementary collars the already constructed $K_B$ copies agree on intersections: on a common quadrant each is the pullback of the same further-face potential. They consequently define a *single* nonnegative function $K_{\rm end}$ on the collar union. Copies are not added together on an intersection. The metric, measure, and $V_A^*$ are likewise exact products there. In particular $V^*$ has the same formula on a face: $C$, $\lambda_{r+1}$, $b_{r+1}$, the label set, and the old torus list do not depend on base dimension, after padding unused flat factors on each finite compared diagram.

For the finitely many elementary collars choose angles $\theta_i$ which rise from $0$ to $\pi/2$ over a normal interval of length $L$, inside the valid common product ranges. Use the squared partition $$\eta_0=\prod_i\cos\theta_i,\qquad
 \eta_i=\sin\theta_i\prod_{a<i}\cos\theta_a.$$ It has $\sum_i\eta_i^2=1$ and $\sum_i|d\eta_i|^2\le\sum_i|d\theta_i|^2\le c_A L^{-2}$; the inequality follows successively from the two-term sine/cosine identity, so no number of intersections is being summed separately. The support of $\eta_0$ lies in a compact collar truncation.

Choose smooth multipliers $\alpha,\beta\in[0,1]$ with $\alpha=1$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\eta_0$ and $\beta=1$ on the union of the face-cutoff supports. Let $\alpha$ vanish only farther out than those transition intervals. Let $\beta$ vanish on the inner side of a buffered collar union, so $\beta K_{\rm end}$ extends smoothly by zero. Both can be formed from products of one-coordinate cutoffs. Define $$K_A=\alpha K_{\rm ind}+\beta K_{\rm end}.$$ On the central support this dominates $K_{\rm ind}$; on each collar support it dominates the single face copy. Eventually $\alpha=0,\beta=1$, giving exact compatibility. Nonnegativity permits the harmless overlap of these two terms in the finite transition region.

This correction vanishes in a uniform neighborhood of $D_A$. The individual correction already does. For the end term, a point of $D_A$ in a product collar projects into $D_B$, because a face has a smaller required counting threshold. Start the support of $\beta$ with a buffer from the collar entry. A point sufficiently close to $D_A$ in this support then has a nearby required point in the same collar, and their face projections are just as close. It lies in the vanishing neighborhood of $K_B$. Take the minimum of the finitely many face widths and entry buffers; at corners the copies agree, so the same conclusion applies.

Apply the individual gap to $\eta_0u$. Apply each face gap by slicing to $\eta_iu$; its support is away from the collar entry, and the normal gradient term is nonnegative. If $g_* =\min(\gamma_A,\{g_B\})>0$, the finite IMS identity gives $$Q_A(u)\ge(g_*-Cc_A L^{-2})\|u\|_2^2.$$ Choose $L$ so that the error is at most $g_*/2$. All quantities used for this choice are fixed before solving any elliptic equation. Boundedness and the derivative assertions follow from compact parts and finitely many product copies. Empty required sets or absent faces cause no change: one may take $\theta=\pi/2$ or omit the corresponding cutoff. This completes the compatible induction and proves the operator identity. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-regular-resolvent"></a>
<div id="regular:resolvent" class="lemma">

**Lemma 67** (The bounded gapped resolvent). *Let $A$ be one of the resulting complete packets, with its weighted base measure. Suppose $W$ is smooth and bounded with bounded derivatives, and $L=-C\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_\omega+W$ has form gap $g>0$. Then there is a unique bounded smooth solution $s$ of $Ls=1$. It satisfies $0<\inf s\le\sup s<\infty$ and has bounded derivatives of every fixed order. Bounded smooth supersolutions of $Lw\ge0$ are nonnegative. The same conclusions hold on Euclidean products of $A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-resolvent"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-resolvent</summary>

*Proof.* Use the bounded geometry, positive bounded weight, and center-uniform polynomial volume estimate of [packet:geometry](#packet:geometry). The form gap and boundedness of $W$ make the closed form coercive on $H^1$: combine $Q\ge g\|u\|_2^2$ with $Q\ge C\|\nabla u\|_2^2-\|W_-\|_\infty\|u\|_2^2$. It therefore has a self-adjoint inverse on $L^2$, of norm at most $g^{-1}$. If $f\ge0$, testing $Lu=f$ with the negative part gives $Q(u_-)\le0$, so the inverse is positive.

Here is the decay estimate needed for bounded sources. We use an Agmon-type exponential-weight argument (Agmon 1982) <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[agmon1982](#ref-agmon1982)], proved here for the weighted packet operator. If $f$ is supported in a metric unit ball $I$ and $u=L^{-1}f$, put $\chi=\exp(\delta\min\{d(\cdot,I),D\})$, where $C\delta^2<g$. The form identity for a bounded Lipschitz multiplier gives $$Q(\chi u)=\langle Lu,\chi^2u\rangle+
                  C\int|\nabla\chi|^2u^2.$$ It holds by compact approximation in $H^1$; on the support of $f$, $\chi=1$. Hence <a id="regular:off-diagonal"></a>$$\label{regular:off-diagonal}
 (g-C\delta^2)\|\chi u\|_2^2
 \le\langle f,u\rangle\le g^{-1}\|f\|_2^2.$$ Let $D\to\infty$. The polynomial volume bound, uniform in the center of $I$, implies $$\int_A e^{-2\delta d(x,I)}\,\mathrm d\mu(x)\le C_{A,\delta}<\infty.$$ Indeed sum the exponential bound on successive unit annuli against their polynomial volume upper bound.

Choose smooth compactly supported $0\le f_R\le1$ equal to $1$ on an increasing compact exhaustion, and put $u_R=L^{-1}f_R\ge0$. These are initially $H^1$ weak solutions. For each fixed $R$, the coefficients and $f_R$ are smooth, and weak interior regularity makes $u_R$ smooth (Simon 2015, Lecture 6, Theorem 1 and Corollary 1) <a id="pm-cite-60"></a>[[simonPDE](#ref-simonPDE)]. This assertion requires no derivative bounds for $f_R$ uniform in $R$.

For any $h\in L^2(I)$, self-adjointness, [regular:off-diagonal](#regular:off-diagonal), and Cauchy–Schwarz with opposite exponential weights give $$|\langle u_R,h\rangle|
 =|\langle f_R,L^{-1}h\rangle|
 \le\frac{C_{A,\delta}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{g(g-C\delta^2)}}\|h\|_2.$$ Thus $u_R$ has a uniform local $L^2$ bound on every unit ball, with constant independent of its center and of $R$. Choose the uniform chart radius small enough that each larger chart ball lies in a metric unit ball. Writing $B_1$ and $B_{1/2}$ for the fixed rescaling of two such nested chart balls, interior local boundedness gives $$\|u_R\|_{L^\infty(B_{1/2})}
 \le c_A\bigl(\|u_R\|_{L^2(B_1)}
                      +\|f_R\|_{L^\infty(B_1)}\bigr).$$ Here we use Simon (2015, Lecture 18, Theorem 2, with $p=2$) <a id="pm-cite-61"></a>[[simonPDE](#ref-simonPDE)]; the fixed radius and coordinate changes are absorbed in $c_A$. The principal coefficients are uniformly elliptic and bounded, the lower-order coefficients are bounded, and the weight is bounded above and below. Since $0\le f_R\le1$, this gives a bound independent of the center and $R$.

Fix two nested relatively compact coordinate domains. For all sufficiently large $R$, the source is exactly $1$ on the larger domain. The interior Schauder estimate (Simon 2015, Lecture 12, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[simonPDE](#ref-simonPDE)], followed by differentiation, therefore bounds every derivative of $u_R$ on the smaller domain, uniformly for these $R$. A diagonal subsequence converges smoothly on compact subsets to a bounded nonnegative solution $s$ of $Ls=1$. Applying the same estimates to this limiting equation in the uniform packet charts gives bounded derivatives of every fixed order on $A$.

The maximum argument must allow $W$ to have either sign. Let $w$ be a bounded smooth supersolution, put $w_-=\max\{-w,0\}$, and fix $0<\delta<\sqrt{g/C}$. Write $\psi=e^{-\delta d(x,x_0)}$ and take compact Lipschitz cutoffs $\zeta_R$ equal to $1$ on $B(x_0,R)$, supported in $B(x_0,2R)$, with $|d\zeta_R|\le 2/R$. Testing the supersolution with $(\psi\zeta_R)^2w_-$, and expanding the gradient, gives directly $$g\|\psi\zeta_R w_-\|_2^2
 \le Q(\psi\zeta_R w_-)
 \le C\int |d(\psi\zeta_R)|^2w_-^2.$$ All tests are compact, so no prior global gradient bound on $w$ is needed. Boundedness of $w$ and polynomial volume growth make $\psi w_-$ square integrable. The terms containing $d\zeta_R$ tend to zero, including the cross term by Cauchy–Schwarz. Dominated convergence therefore yields $$g\|\psi w_-\|_2^2\le C\delta^2\|\psi w_-\|_2^2,$$ so $w_-=0$. On an open subdomain the same compact-test argument applies when the negative part has zero boundary trace, using the Dirichlet form gap. In particular it applies to nonnegative boundary values. Apply comparison to both signs of the difference of two bounded solutions to obtain uniqueness. If $M=1+\|W\|_\infty$, then $L(s-M^{-1})=1-W/M\ge0$, and comparison gives $s\ge M^{-1}$.

Euclidean products retain bounded geometry and polynomial volume, and their form gap follows by slicing. The preceding proof therefore applies verbatim; uniqueness identifies their solution with the pullback of $s$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-regular-solutions"></a>
<div id="regular:solutions" class="lemma">

**Lemma 68** (Exact solutions and compatible approximations). *Fix *all* the corrections of [regular:correction](#regular:correction) and let $s_A$ denote the exact bounded solution $L_As_A=1$. There exist positive smooth compatible functions $v_A$ with <a id="regular:strict-supersolution"></a>$$\label{regular:strict-supersolution}
 L_Av_A\ge\tfrac12$$ on every packet. They have positive infimum and bounded derivatives packetwise. These $v_A$ are approximations; they are not relabeled as the original exact solutions $s_A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-solutions"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-solutions</summary>

*Proof.* We first establish a uniform end estimate over a whole face, which also avoids any compactness ambiguity at its deeper ends. On an exact elementary collar $B\times[T,\infty)$, the difference $w=s_A-s_B$ is bounded and satisfies $L_Aw=0$. Set $m_B=\inf s_B>0$ and choose $a>0$ with $Ca^2\|s_B\|_\infty\le1/2$. The function $$b(x,t)=e^{-a(t-T)}s_B(x)$$ satisfies $L_Ab=e^{-a(t-T)}(1-Ca^2s_B)\ge\tfrac12e^{-a(t-T)}$. Comparison on the half collar, using its sliced Dirichlet gap and the Dirichlet comparison argument in the proof of [regular:resolvent](#regular:resolvent), yields <a id="regular:end-decay"></a>$$\label{regular:end-decay}
 |s_A-s_B|(x,t)\le
 \frac{\|s_A-s_B\|_\infty}{m_B}\,
 e^{-a(t-T)}s_B(x).$$ The boundary inequalities hold at $t=T$ for both signs. Uniform interior elliptic estimates for the homogeneous difference give the same exponential decay in $C^2$ on $t\ge T+1$. All constants are uniform in $x\in B$, including its deeper ends. Thus the estimate holds on each elementary collar, also where collars overlap. Exact agreement of the approximations on those overlaps will come from the compatible face copies used in the following construction.

All operator coefficients and every exact $s_A$ are now fixed. Let $M_A$ be a finite bound for $L_A:C^2\to C^0$ in uniform charts, and choose a target $$\tau_A<\min\{\tfrac14\inf s_A,\ (4M_A)^{-1}\}.$$ The finite family of collar blending cutoffs has a $C^2$ operator bound $H_A\ge1$, independent of translation of their starts. Include in $H_A$ the fixed finite factor for the number of copies. For each packet $B$, let $\varepsilon_B$ be the minimum of $$\frac{\tau_A}{\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}(4H_{A_i})}
 \qquad
 (A=A_0>A_1>\cdots>A_\ell=B),$$ over all directed coface-to-face chains ending at $B$, including the length-zero chain. There are finitely many such chains, so this minimum is positive. Appending the edge $A>B$ to a minimizing chain for $A$ gives $\varepsilon_B\le\varepsilon_A/(4H_A)$. All these numbers are fixed after the corrections and operator norms, and before constructing any $v_A$.

Construct $v_A$ from deeper faces upward. Keep $s_A$ in the central part and use the ordinary weights in the proof of [regular:partition](#regular:partition) to blend to the already constructed copies of $v_B$. Move the starts until $\|s_A-s_B\|_{C^2}<\varepsilon_A/(4H_A)$ on all participating collars, using [regular:end-decay](#regular:end-decay). The persistent difference $v_B-s_B$ is paid by its previously allocated tolerance, not by moving the start. Start also beyond the finitely many compatibility thresholds for the $v_B$’s, so their copies agree on overlaps. The derivative bounds for the weights then give $$\|v_A-s_A\|_{C^2}\le\varepsilon_A\le\tau_A.$$ The result is an exact face copy sufficiently far out, is smooth, and has bounded higher derivatives by compact parts and finitely many smooth products. Finally $v_A\ge\tfrac34\inf s_A>0$ and $L_Av_A\ge1-M_A\tau_A\ge3/4$, which implies [regular:strict-supersolution](#regular:strict-supersolution). No geometry radius, correction, or operator is changed during this approximation. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-43"></a>
#### Restoring the invariant

The passage from a positive solution to scalar-curvature control by a circle warp appears in Fischer-Colbrie and Schoen (1980, Theorem 4<span class="upright">(ii)</span>) <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[fischerColbrieSchoen1980](#ref-fischerColbrieSchoen1980)]. Here bounded positive supersolutions must also be exactly compatible with every packet end; [regular:solutions](#regular:solutions) supplies these functions.

<a id="stmt-regular-step"></a>
<div id="regular:step" class="proposition">

**Proposition 69** (The full invariant step). *Assume the stage $r$ invariant and the conclusion of [shield:global](#shield:global). For every nonempty parent packet of base dimension at least two, processing $j_0$ produces the compatible smooth split packets of [packet:split](#packet:split), with the required forced signs and continuous projection maps. There are smooth compatible old metrics and $399$ new torus factors on every packet such that the profiles, cores, potentials, and scalar curvature satisfy the stage $r+1$ invariant. This includes a possible one-dimensional output, on which the scalar lower bound is global. After the radial split the packets have the complete compact-part/product-end geometry of [packet:geometry](#packet:geometry).*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-regular-step"><summary>Proof of stmt-regular-step</summary>

*Proof.* Use [regular:partition](#regular:partition), [regular:seam](#regular:seam), [regular:profiles](#regular:profiles) for the smooth base metrics and the profile data, and [regular:correction](#regular:correction), [regular:solutions](#regular:solutions) for $v_A$. Append $k=399$ circle variables, each with length function $v_A^{2/(k+1)}$. The warped-product identity is $$R_{\rm new}=R_{\rm old}-\frac{4k}{k+1}
                    \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm old}v_A}{v_A}.$$ Indeed a common scale $f$ contributes $-2k f^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm old}f-k(k-1)f^{-2}|df|^2$; substitution of $f=v_A^{2/(k+1)}$ cancels the separate $|dv_A|^2$ term. The old Laplacian on invariant functions is the weighted base Laplacian, including the product of all old warp lengths. Here $k$ counts only the newly appended circle factors. Any unit factors used earlier to compare old lists on a finite diagram have zero logarithmic derivative and leave both $\mathop{\mathrm{\Delta}}_{\rm old}$ and this coefficient unchanged. Since $4k/(k+1)=C$, [regular:Vstar](#regular:Vstar), [regular:strict-supersolution](#regular:strict-supersolution) give $$R_{\rm new}=V_A^*-K_A+\frac{L_Av_A}{v_A}
 \ge V_A^*\quad\hbox{on }D_A.$$ Here $K_A=0$ on a neighborhood of $D_A$. Adding these factors does not change base distances or profile gradients. The new factors are positive, bounded above and away from zero, and exactly compatible. The same number is appended in every status at the stage, so all end identities compare the same list of factors. This proves the analytic invariant, including when the new required threshold is zero. The componentwise metric operations preserve every unused old circle length identically equal to $1$: restriction, product extension, and $\beta_s\mapsto\beta_{\phi(x,s)}$ all fix that constant entry. The $399$ appended lengths are the only common new entries. Likewise restriction and cylinder pullback preserve a constant pole profile and its full core, and [regular:constant-preservation](#regular:constant-preservation) preserves the specified multiplied constant potentials.

The zero wall lies in $\operatorname{int}G_{j_0}$ and all current forced signs hold by [regular:partition](#regular:partition); earlier forced cores are carried by the continuous projection and retain their pole neighborhoods. Thus this is exactly the regular compatible splitting operation required by the packet construction. At the radial split, the preserved alternating compact barrier bands separate all wall components into bounded transition shells and trap every closed sign-side component between two separating bands (with a compact initial block). These components are compact before prolongation. The normal collars and smooth metrics used above are products in all old ends. [packet:split](#packet:split), [packet:geometry](#packet:geometry) therefore supply the claimed geometry after prolongation. The main argument applies the one-dimensional obstruction and reconstructs the compatible partitions in the original manifold. ◻

</details>

      "caption": "A regular cut retains each closed sign side with a full cylinder attached, and also retains the wall as a separate hit space of one lower base dimension.       "pdf_page": null,
      "source_evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "A regular cut retains each closed sign side with a full cylinder\nattached, and also retains the wall as a separate hit space of one lower base\ndimension. The attachment seam is internal to the extended side. In old\nproduct ends this entire ",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,>=Stealth,font=\\small]\n  \\fill[blue!7] (0,0) rectangle (2.3,2.2);\n  \\fill[orange!12] (2.3,0) rectangle (5,2.2);\n  \\draw[thick] (0,0) -- (5,0);\n  \\draw[thick] (0,2.2) -- (5,2.2);\n  \\draw[densely dashed,thick] (2.3,0) -- (2.3,2.2);\n  \\node[align=center] at (1.1,1.2) {retained\\\\closed side};\n  \\node[align=center] at (3.6,1.2) {$L\\times[0,\\infty)$\\\\attached\\\\cylinder};\n  \\draw[->,thick] (4.4,.5) -- (5.3,.5) node[right] {$s$};\n  \\node[align=center,below] at (2.3,-.1) {internal attachment seam};\n  \\draw[very thick,teal!70!black] (7,0) -- (7,2.2);\n  \\node[align=center,right] at (7.2,1.1) {separate hit space $L$\\\\base dimension $m-1$};\n  \\node[above] at (2.5,2.7) {either sign side: base dimension $m$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "ABSENT"
    },
    {

          "exact_quote": "The two maps used in a finite reconstruction, shown schematically\nin local normal coordinates: $s$ on the reconstructed domain $X_{r+1}$\nand $t$ on the old target $\\widehat X_r$. The target strip $|t|<\\delta$\nrepresents the chosen collar $S",

      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1.25cm,y=1.05cm,>=Latex,font=\\small]\n \\fill[blue!6] (-3,-1) rectangle (3,1);\n \\fill[red!8] (-1,-2.25) rectangle (1,2.35);\n \\draw[->] (-3.2,0)--(3.5,0) node[right] {$s$};\n \\draw[->] (0,-2.3)--(0,2.65) node[above] {$t$};\n \\draw[densely dashed,gray] (-3,1)--(3,1);\n \\draw[densely dashed,gray] (-3,-1)--(3,-1);\n \\draw[very thick,purple!75!black]\n  (-3,-2)--(-1,0)--(1,0)--(3,2);\n \\draw[thick,orange!85!black]\n  (-3,-2)--(-2,-1)--(-1,-.35)--(0,0)--(1,.35)--(2,1)--(3,2);\n \\draw (-1,.07)--(-1,-.07) node[below=4pt,fill=white,inner sep=1pt] {$-L$};\n \\draw (1,.07)--(1,-.07) node[below=4pt] {$L$};\n \\node[left,fill=white,inner sep=1pt] at (-.08,1) {$\\delta$};\n \\node[left,fill=white,inner sep=1pt] at (-.08,-1) {$-\\delta$};\n \\node[align=left,anchor=west,text=purple!75!black] at (3.55,1.25)\n  {collapse $c_r$};\n \\node[align=left,anchor=west,text=orange!85!black] at (3.55,.55)\n  {compression $h_r$};\n \\node[align=left,anchor=west] at (3.55,-.55)\n  {$c_r=h_r$ outside the strip};\n \\node[align=left,anchor=west] at (3.55,-1.35)\n  {old strata follow\\\\the same reparameterization};\n \\node[below,align=center] at (0,-2.4)\n  {finite inserted cylinder: $-L\\le s\\le L$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
